# pob2-mcp: spec spine

**Status:** Folded through amendment 16 (approved; as built through 15 and amendment 16's M-29), 2026-11-09. This one
spine replaces the project's two spines:
- the **server spine**, `docs/stabilization/spec/README.md`, v2 of 2026-10-03. The user ratified it on 2026-10-04,
  with every PROPOSED decision as written (`docs/stabilization/build-handoff.md`:19), though its status line still says
  "pending the user's ratification";
- the **build-guide spine**, `docs/build-guide/spec/README.md`, v1.6 of 2026-10-18, folded through amendment 7
  (D-397), with D-398 and D-399 joined the same day.

Both old spines stay as the rationale record, as do the amendment files (`docs/build-guide/spec/amendment-*.md`); this
spine is authoritative. It merges the two and folds amendments 8 to 14 (D-400…D-495, M-20…M-27), all approved and
built, of which the build-guide spine held only the rows edited in place (listed below). The user approved the merge
and the fold on 2026-11-04 ("Go ahead"). Amendment 15 (D-499…D-506, M-28), which the user approved on 2026-11-06, was
built the same day; its build's last unit, WU-149, marked its rows built (the fold rule, below): D-499…D-505 built,
D-506 approved as the roadmap (amendments 16 to 18 build it), M-28 built; the user's install check (HG-37) passed on 2026-11-07 (WL:6827–6872).
Amendment 16 (D-507…D-525, M-29 and M-30), which the user approved on 2026-11-08 as revision 2 with its seven choices
answered (WL:6985–7004), is built in two milestones. M-29 was built on 2026-11-08 and 2026-11-09 (WL:7030 on); its docs
unit, WU-158, marked M-29's rows built (the fold rule's middle step, below): D-507…D-515 built, D-525's M-29 part
built, M-29 built; WU-157 (the agent types' probe, AC-W140) not run, recorded by WU-168 at the latest. M-30's rows stay
approved until its docs unit, WU-167, marks them built.

**How to read.**
- This spine is the **single source of truth** for what crosses slices: the interface contracts (C-1…C-21 from the
  server, C-30…C-66 from the build-guide, C-54 withdrawn), the data and state model, the decision log (D-1…D-92,
  D-100…D-525), the autonomy envelope (assumptions A-1…A-15 and A-20 on, human-decision gates HG-1…HG-39), the
  milestones (M-0…M-3 with M-α, M-10…M-18, M-20…M-30), the work-unit graph (WU-01…WU-25, WU-30…WU-168), the
  guardrails, the risk register (R-1…R-14, R-20 on), the test strategy and the cross-cutting criteria (AC-B). No ID
  collides between the two old spines: D-206 numbered the build-guide's milestones from M-10. "M-19" is the
  coordinator's label (in `docs/build-guide/build-handoff-amendments.md`) for the 2026-10-18 fix batch and the v1.6
  fold, run together; it is not a spine milestone.
- **When a slice and this spine disagree, the spine wins**; fold the fix back here. The slices are folded only as far
  as §4 says. Until a slice is folded, the amendment sections §4 lists for it hold its current text. Where an
  amendment and the work-log's as-built record differ, the as-built record wins and the difference is stated beside
  the row.
- **The decision rows of amendments 8 to 16 (D-400…D-495, D-499…D-525) are summaries.** Each cites its amendment's
  section, which holds the decision's full text: its formulas, conditions, exceptions and the user's rulings. Where a
  row says less than its section, the section holds; where a row contradicts its section, the row is a fold error, to
  be fixed here. This holds until the slices those sections change are folded (§4).
- **Reading rule for build agents.** Read the server's conventions (§3.1.2), this spine, then your unit's slice spec
  and any amendment section §4 lists against it. Cite IDs in commits and the work-log. Anything no contract or
  decision settles is either a proceed-on assumption (§8.2) or a human-decision gate (§8.3). Never invent a third
  category.
- A passage marked *target* describes approved behaviour that isn't built yet. The approved but unbuilt items are the
  human units (the server's WU-20 items and WU-21, M-27's live test WU-144, M-29's probe WU-157 with AC-W140 and
  HG-38; the live alpha WU-48 was retired, D-498) and amendment 16's M-30 rows (D-516…D-524, D-525's M-30 part,
  C-61…C-66, HG-39, M-30, WU-159…WU-168, R-123…R-130), approved 2026-11-08. A "target" in a row's history that names a
  built amendment, or amendment 16's M-29, is built.
- Amended rows keep their text and add `**amended by D-nnn (…)**: <gist>`: "(v1.5)" and "(v1.6)" mark the build-guide
  spine's folds, "(amendment N, as built)" this spine's, and "(amendment N, approved)" an approved amendment not yet
  built, which its build's last unit rewords as built; amendment 16's M-29 notes read "(amendment 16, as built)" since
  WU-158, its M-30 notes "(amendment 16, approved)" until WU-167. The source shorthands rows use (WL:n, worklog:n, recon §n,
  gap n, review Sn, F-n, pin §, platform §, ext §, S-N1, S-N2, PG-n) are defined in §4.3.

**Capability glossary** (the design's IDs): K1 game-data lookup · K2 model-coverage detection · M1
offense breakdown and skill-part selection · M2 ranker amendments · L1 set and state authoring · P1
build-plan format · P2 plan compiler · C1 campaign reference data · E1 enemy reference profiles · T1
dated pricing · V1 stage validity check · V2 config check · V4 claim verification (V3, the provenance
check, was removed by D-268) · R1 research (R1a parallel lookup-only agents, R1b the coordinator's
compile, measure and price) · D1 endgame design · D2 campaign plan · W1 guide writer · O1 orchestration,
now the runner: the user's session following `SKILL.md` (D-267).

**The fold rule** (the user's direction, 2026-11-04; its status line in two steps since amendment 15 §13, with a middle
step for a two-milestone amendment since a16 §21). This spine never falls behind an approved amendment:
- when the user approves an amendment, the approval commit enters its spine rows (decisions, contracts, envelope rows,
  its milestone, work units and risks) with status approved, and moves the status line to "Folded through amendment
  <n> (approved; as built through <n−1>)";
- an amendment built in two milestones adds a middle step: the first milestone's docs unit marks that milestone's rows
  built and moves the status line to "Folded through amendment <n> (approved; as built through <n−1> and amendment
  <n>'s M-<m>)", M-<m> being that first milestone (a16 §21);
- the last unit of its build updates those rows to as built (the work-log's record wins over the amendment's text, the
  difference stated beside the row) and drops the status line's parenthesis, so it reads "Folded through amendment
  <n>";
- `tests/static/specSpine.test.ts` fails when the spine falls behind: when a decision an approved amendment's headings
  name has no row in §7, when the status doesn't name the newest approved amendment, when a row of either old spine is
  missing here, or when an old spine doesn't say at its top that it is superseded by this one;
- when a later approved decision contradicts an older one without naming it, the older row is marked amended by it,
  unless the coordinator judges the newer one problematic, which then goes to the user (D-496);
- the slices are folded separately, and §4 records how far each one is.

**Where the old spines' sections went.**

| 2 | Problem and goals | 1 | 2 |

| 4 | Slices and documents | slice table, inputs | slice table, inputs |
| 5 | Interface contracts | 3 | 3 (3.1 → 5.1, 3.2 → 5.2) |
| 6 | Data and state model | 4 | 4 |
| 7 | Decision log | 6 | 5 |
| 8 | Autonomy envelope | 7 (7.1–7.3 → 8.1–8.3) | 6 (6.1–6.3 → 8.1–8.3) |
| 9 | Milestones and alpha | 5 | 7 |

milestones' scope and exit criteria are in §9.

### 1.4 Next

- **From amendment 15: the whole skill split** (`docs/design/2026-10-18-skill-split.md`).
  The user accepted the design on 2026-10-18 with its §6 options, "game knowledge first, right after amendment 8 is
  built" (WL:4455–4462); each extraction becomes its own amendment once the user picks it (the design's status line).
  On 2026-11-04 the user scheduled it next ("I want to implement the skill split doc before doing anything else"),
  after this fold, which the user approved first ("Go ahead"). Then: "I don't have a preference for skill split order;
  I won't be doing another run until it's all finished" (WL:6478). So every extraction comes before the next live run,
  in the coordinator's order, the design's "game knowledge first" no longer binding.
- **M-28 built** (amendment 15, 2026-11-06): the repository as a Claude Code plugin, the rules every runner follows
  in one shared file, and the game-knowledge piece. Signed onto `main` and pushed on
  2026-11-07; the user's install and read check (HG-37, WU-150) passed the same day.
- **Amendment 16** (approved 2026-11-08), in two milestones. **M-29 built** (2026-11-08 and 2026-11-09), what the 1.0
  launch on 2026-12-11 needs and the fixes every piece shares: league choice by running leagues (D-507), the scripted
  pin upgrade (D-508), the bridge's patches for PG-106, PG-109 and PG-114 (D-509…D-512), and the small fixes
  (D-513…D-515). Next for it: the milestone gate, then the signing onto `main` with the user (HG-8), before M-30

## 2. Problem and goals

### 2.1 The server (from the stabilization spine)

The goals and non-goals are in [vision §1–2](../stabilization/vision.md). In short, make pob2-mcp a stable,
PoE2-correct MCP server:

| Goal | Statement | Proven by |
|---|---|---|
| G-1 | The engine connection never silently loses, corrupts or mis-attributes build state | AC-E5…AC-E23, AC-E46, AC-E55…AC-E64 |
| G-2 | Every registered tool is PoE2-correct; PoE1-only tools are removed | AC-F18…AC-F22, AC-F25, AC-G6 |
| G-3 | Advice reports what PoB2 computes: every judgment is a PoB warning or a comparison of engine outputs | AC-A1…AC-A6 |
| G-4 | `find_upgrades` (slot, budget, required mods, weighted sort stats) works end to end and returns actionable results | AC-T1…AC-T45, WU-20 |
| G-5 | A local gate fails loudly on any regression, including when the engine can't run | AC-F9, AC-F17, AC-F23…AC-F26 |

**Out of scope** (do not build):
- PoB's unique-jewel trade rows: deferred by the user (HUMAN) to a follow-up epic once the core is stable;
- multi-slot budget optimisation;
- crafting simulation;
- new analysis features;
- hosted CI;
- a PoB pin bump;
- MCP `structuredContent`/`outputSchema` (D-36);
- an SDK upgrade.

### 2.2 The build-guide (from the build-guide spine)

The user has played PoE1 and PoE2 for years but has never built a character from scratch. They want a
workflow that takes a brief and produces a written guide plus a PoB2 build that a newcomer can follow from
level 1 through endgame (D-101). The brief is any mix of an ascendancy, a skill, a playstyle, a budget, an
item or mechanic to build around, a reference build, or almost nothing; a randomizer roll of an ascendancy
and a main skill (first: Titan / Blood Hunt) is one case (D-285, target). The user is the guide's reader,
not its fact-checker, so the workflow checks its own claims and says plainly when it guesses (D-105,
D-142). PoB2 leaves much unmodelled: 12.9% of unique mod lines, about 30% of keystones and 32% of
ascendancy notables carry a line PoB ignores (pin §D), so hand calculations are routine.

Since amendment 9 (D-411, D-415), a build's guide is the endgame guide by default: the reader levels with any build
of the class, then switches to the build's endgame rungs; the full guide, from level 1 through the campaign, adds
the campaign on request (the final gate's `campaign`).

| Goal | Statement | Proven by |
|---|---|---|
| G-10 | The server exposes PoB2's loaded game data, per-skill and per-skill-part numbers, and what PoB can't model (K1, K2, M1, M2) | AC-K |
| G-11 | The server can author every piece of a stage's state: sets, loadouts, weapon-set passives, attribute choices, tree jewels, flask toggles, a consistent main skill (L1) | AC-K |
| G-12 | A plan compiles into one PoB2 file with a loadout per stage, a compile record, a re-evaluated ledger, an import code, and V1/V2 verdicts (P1, P2, C1, E1, V1, V2) | AC-P, NFR-20, NFR-21 |
| G-13 | The pipeline produces the dossier, plan, record and guide, every claim tagged with its source and checked by V4, with the findings still open stated in the guide (R1, D1, D2, W1, V4, O1; D-268). Since WU-62 (D-276, D-278), the guide renders the plan without markers, the plan's claims carrying the sources, with `audit.md` beside it. Since WU-57 (D-287), the brainstorm adds the cards and the deck. Since M-15 (D-351, D-352), claims are written only for what the build hinges on, checked by one V4 pass. Since M-18 (D-389), every gate is an interview: the user's questions are answered before the decision | AC-W, M-12, M-14, M-15, M-18 |
| G-14 | The Titan / Blood Hunt guide is one the user can actually level and map with | M-13 sign-off; retired with M-13 (D-498) |
| G-15 | Nothing existing regresses: tool contracts, golden values, the offline rules | AC-B |


## 3. Existing system context (historical)

Each epic described the system as it found it: the server as of the stabilization recon, the build-guide as of its
recon of `e6ebec1`. Neither subsection is updated as the code moves on; the current tool counts and the state of
the build are in §1, the contracts in §5.

### 3.1 The server (from the stabilization spine)

Detail and citations are in [grounding/ctx-ts.md](../stabilization/grounding/ctx-ts.md) and
[grounding/ctx-lua.md](../stabilization/grounding/ctx-lua.md). Each slice spec repeats the parts it touches.

#### 3.1.1 Components in scope

| Component | Location | Today |
|---|---|---|
| Server entry + class | `src/index.ts` (404 lines) | Low-level SDK `Server` (1.20.2). **Instantiates itself at import** (`:403-404`). Fetches trade2 `/data/stats` before `connect()` when trade is on. Installs process listeners in its constructor. No stdin-EOF handling. Ignores the MCP request `extra` |
| Tool schemas | `src/server/toolSchemas.ts` (1810) | 10 `get*ToolSchemas()` groups of JSON-Schema literals. 98 registered tools; listing is gated by env flags in `index.ts` |
| Router | `src/server/toolRouter.ts` (773) | `routeToolCall(name,args,deps)`: one `switch` over 100 names. It builds 8 contexts eagerly on every call. Routing ignores registration |
| Engine manager | `src/server/luaClientManager.ts` (104) | Lazy `ensureClient` (not single-flight). Silent restart. Its readiness probe loads a PoE1-root XML |
| Bridge client | `src/pobLuaBridge.ts` (555) | Spawns luajit with the global `spawn`. Request `{action,params}`, no ids. A per-line timeout that doesn't kill the process. 38 methods |
| Handlers | `src/handlers/*.ts` (22 files) | `handle*` functions wrapped in `wrapHandler`. Return `{content:[{type:"text",text}]}`. Throw on error. At least 7 paths return error text as success |
| Services | `src/services/*.ts` (15) | Build parsing and cache (60 s TTL, keyed by the name as passed), tree data, trade client (Bottleneck, global `fetch`), poe.ninja client (global `fetch`), export/snapshot, watcher (chokidar 4.0.3), … |
| Utils | `src/utils/*.ts` | `contextBuilder.ts`, `errorHandling.ts` (`wrapHandler`), `pathSanitizer.ts` (`sanitizeBuildName`), `buildCode.ts` (global `fetch`, browser-like UA) |
| Lua bridge | `pob-api/bootstrap.lua`, `pob-api/API/{Server,Handlers,BuildOps}.lua` | Vanilla PoB2 via `HeadlessWrapper.lua`. Newline JSON over stdio. 39 handlers + `quit`. Encoding happens outside `pcall` |
| PoB2 engine | sibling dev-head checkout, via `POB_FORK_PATH` | **Not pinned** today. The default `~/Projects/PathOfBuilding-PoE2/src` doesn't exist on this box |
| Tests | `tests/**`, `jest.config.cjs`, `tsconfig.test.json` | 274 tests in 17 suites (4 at the `tests/` root). ts-jest compiles `src` as CommonJS. The integration suite passes with no assertions when the engine is absent (E10). There are coverage thresholds but no setup files |
| Toolchain | `mise.toml`, `package.json`, `pnpm-lock.yaml` | mise: LuaJIT ref `2460b3ff` + luautf8, Node 24. pnpm 11.6.0. No mise tasks |

#### 3.1.2 Conventions every unit follows

- **Modules.** ESM TypeScript, Node16. Relative imports end in `.js`. `import type` for types. Default
  imports for Node built-ins. **No `import.meta`, `__dirname` or top-level await in `src/`**, because Jest
  compiles `src` as CommonJS (TS1343). New helpers go in the existing **`src/utils/`** directory.
- **Tools.** A tool is a JSON-Schema literal in `toolSchemas.ts`, a `case` in `toolRouter.ts`, and
  `handle<Pascal>(context, …)` in `src/handlers/<area>Handlers.ts`, wrapped with
  `wrapHandler('<verb phrase>', …)`. Handlers fail by **throwing**.
- **Output and logging.** Output is plain text with `===` headers. Log only to stderr, as
  `console.error('[Component] …')`. **stdout is reserved for MCP.**
- **Paths and env.** Paths under the builds dir go through `sanitizeBuildName(name, baseDir)` and, for
  writes and session keys, `canonicalBuildPath` (D-52). Env is read only in `src/index.ts`; everything
  else gets it through `deps.env` (C-7).
- **Lua.** `local M = {} … return M`. BuildOps returns `value` or `nil, '<lowercase message>'`, and
  Handlers maps that to `{ok=false,error}` / `{ok=true,<key>=…}`. Newer handlers use the key `result`.
  Actions are snake_case and params camelCase. `pob-api/` uses two-space indentation.
- **Tests.** Tests live under `tests/` (`unit/`, `mcp/`, `integration/`, `e2e/`, `static/`; four legacy
  suites sit at the root) and import from `@jest/globals`.

#### 3.1.3 Existing state in scope (as it is today)

| State | Location today | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| User builds | `POB_DIRECTORY` (this user: `/mnt/c/Users/parke/Documents/Path of Building (PoE2)/Builds`, Windows drvfs, **case-insensitive**) | System of record. The server writes there only via C-17 |
| Snapshots, `save_tree` backups, exports | `<POB_DIRECTORY>/.pob-mcp/{snapshots,exports}/` (`.xml` + `_metadata.json`) | **Moves** to the state dir, C-16 (D-18), in WU-22 |
| Config presets | `<POB_DIRECTORY>/.pob-mcp-presets/<name>.json` | **Moves** to the state dir, C-16 (D-18), in WU-22 |
| Engine build state | inside luajit, lost on restart | Snapshot after each change; restore on restart (C-3) |
| Caches | build parse 60 s/20 (name-keyed); tree data ∞; trade cache (unbounded, ignores TTL); poe.ninja 5 min | Build parse cache validated by size+mtime (D-58); trade per C-10; poe.ninja per C-12 |

#### 3.1.4 Prior decisions in scope

[Vision](../stabilization/vision.md) D-1…D-17 are ratified and are recorded in §7, with the refinements
formalization made. The user's answers during formalization are D-18, D-19 and D-20. D-21 onwards are
coordinator decisions (PROPOSED) from formalization and the spec review. D-61 is a user decision made
during the build. D-62 onwards fold the build's recorded deviations back into the spec (PROPOSED (build)).

### 3.2 The build-guide (from the build-guide spine)

Summarized from recon (an audit of `e6ebec1`), which holds the file:line evidence. Nothing here is
redefined; §5 tags every contract this epic relies on or changes.

#### 3.2.1 Components in scope

| Component | Today (recon §1) | Changed by |

| Tool wiring: `toolSchemas.ts`, `toolMeta.ts`, `toolRouter.ts`, listing in `createServer.ts` | 77 `TOOL_META` entries (kinds `none`, `read`, `mutate`, `load`, `lifecycle`; groups `core`, `trade`, `ninja`). A tool without an entry is unlisted and unroutable. Unknown tool → −32602. Handler errors → `isError` | every tool-adding unit (§9) |
| Engine session `src/engine/session.ts` | One FIFO lock per tool call, any kind. Snapshot (a full XML export) after each successful `mutate`/`load`. A ctx load (`newBuild`, `loadFile`) drops the previous build's snapshot (`:778`), so a crash before the call's own snapshot takes the no-snapshot path (`RESTORE_FAILED`, `:230`, `:589`). `isDirty()` and `loaded` are public. Only `find_upgrades` has a deadline | none; `compile_plan` is one call (D-156) |
| Bridge client `src/pobLuaBridge.ts`; reply schemas `src/engine/schemas.ts` (43 actions) | 30 s per request, SIGKILL on timeout. Every reply is validated; a missing schema is a compile error | new actions (C-2/C-5 rows) |
| Lua: `pob-api/API/{Server,Handlers,BuildOps}.lua`; `Trade.lua` merged by one line at `Handlers.lua:493` | `pcall` dispatch; BuildOps returns `value` or `nil,'msg'` | new Lua modules, merged the same way |
| `buildFileWriter` `src/services/buildFileWriter.ts` | Canonical path, explicit overwrite, changed-on-disk guard, backup, temp file + rename | used by `compile_plan` (C-17) |
| Trade: `rateLimiter.ts`, `tradeClient.ts`, price tools | Waits over 30 s → `would_wait`. No price or listing cache. `get_item_price` names the league, gives no date | C-49 (D-202) |
| `lua_get_stats` | `category:'offense'` gives 38 main-skill fields; values are `mainOutput` scalars only; on the 5000-char truncation list | C-45 |
| `set_main_skill` → Lua `set_main_selection` | Writes PoE1's `srcInstance.skillPart`, not PoB2's `statSet`; `skill_part` is an undocumented router alias | C-44 |
| `search_tree_nodes` | `node_type:'any'` matches nothing; the loop stops at the cap before sorting; no ascendancy filter; query required; cap 30 | C-43 |

| `get_passive_upgrades`, `suggest_supports` | D-77, D-78: ascendancy notables excluded; unmeasured candidates sort as −∞ and fall off | C-47 |
| `lua_set_tree`, `update_tree_delta` | Lua neither auto-paths nor caps ascendancy points; the TS side reads `autoPathedNodes`, `skippedAscendancyNodes`, `ascendancyPointsUsed`, which Lua never sends | C-48 |
| Specs; item sets | Spec tools exist. Item sets: list and select only. No skill-set or config-set operations at all | C-39 |

| `src/utils/buildCode.ts` | Decode only; an encoder exists only in tests (`toolHygiene.test.ts:95`) | C-42 |
| `plan_leveling` data, `export_build_summary` | Hand-written table without area or quest data (not reused, D-131); summary without import code | unchanged |
| `.claude/` | 8 agent-os agents and 6 commands; no skills, no workflows | slice 3: the build-guide skill and its four agent types; no workflows (D-267) |

#### 3.2.2 Conventions every unit follows

- **§3.1.2:** ESM with Node16 resolution, relative imports ending in `.js`, `import type`; no
  `import.meta`, `__dirname` or top-level `await` in `src/`; log only to stderr as
  `console.error('[Component] …')`; only `src/index.ts` reads `process.env`; Lua in `pob-api/` uses
  two-space indentation and `local M = {} … return M`; `vendor/` is never modified.
- **A new tool is 4 code edits plus 4 fixture and doc edits** (recon §2): the schema literal, the
  `TOOL_META` entry, the router `case`, `handle<Name>` wrapped in `wrapHandler`; a `tool-disposition.json`
  entry (`bucket:"new"`); at least one `expect:"ok"` sample in `tool-calls.json`; the README table
  regenerated with `pnpm build && pnpm docs:tools --write` (D-83); the count sites (§3.2.3).
- **Checks that already apply to every tool:** router and registry agree; output text never contains
  `undefined`, `NaN` or `[object Object]`; descriptions are ≤ 2048 chars; engine kinds are verified by
  `engineKinds.test.ts` (a `read` sample leaves the canonical export and `revision` unchanged; a
  `mutate`/`load` sample changes both; a sample may declare `stateChange:false` with a reason).
- **Static scans with empty waiver files:** the PoE1-cruft scan over every string literal in
  `src/**/*.ts` (bans PoE1 boss names, `Shadow`, …); the advice-threshold scan over `src/advice/*`,
  `validationHandlers.ts`, `optimizationHandlers.ts`, `buildGoalsHandlers.ts` (bans `quest points`, …);
  `deletedPlumbing`; no dead modules; explicit env in every test spawn.
- **Every new Lua action** gets a C-5 zod schema, a typed bridge wrapper and a `contract.test.ts` case in
  the same merge. Actions are snake_case, params camelCase.
- **Dependencies today:** zod 3, fast-xml-parser, chokidar, the SDK; `zod-to-json-schema` is a
  devDependency. There is no YAML parser and no runtime JSON-Schema validator.

#### 3.2.3 Existing data and state in scope

- **Build XML** (recon §4). Build-wide: `<Build level characterLevelAutoMode mainSocketGroup>`. Per tree
  spec: `<WeaponSet1>`/`<WeaponSet2 nodes>` (weapon-set tags), `<Sockets><Socket nodeId itemId>` (tree
  jewels), `<Overrides><AttributeOverride strNodes dexNodes intNodes>` (attribute choices). Per skill set:
  gems with a per-gem stat-set choice. Per item set: `useSecondWeaponSet`, `<Slot name itemId active>`.
  Per config set: only non-default `<Input>`s are saved (pin `Classes/ConfigTab.lua:739`).
- **State dir** (C-16): `$XDG_STATE_HOME/pob2-mcp/` with `snapshots/`, `backups/`, `exports/`,
  `presets/`; the recovery snapshot lives in memory (D-44). This epic adds one file there: the write
  ledger `guide-writes.json` (D-211).

  (`engineTree.test.ts:86`); Life 2,977 (`fresh-clone-check.mjs:123`).

#### 3.2.4 Prior decisions in scope

- **Respected unchanged:** D-8/D-59 (a lock per call; only the session starts the engine), D-17 (the gate),
  D-18 (no server state in the builds folder), D-19 (disk-change policy), D-22/D-47 (only `find_upgrades` has
  a deadline; 30 s per engine request), D-26 (zod v3, never passed to the SDK), D-27 (handler errors are
  `isError`), D-36 (text output only), D-40 (no automatic retries), D-45 (strict hygiene allowlist), D-46
  (trade responses never cached), D-48 (unsigned commits during the build), D-52 (canonical build paths), D-83
  (generated README table), C-17 with D-60/D-80/D-86 (the writer).
- **Amended or scoped here:** D-77 and D-78 (by D-143, C-47); C-1 and the D-64/D-91 counts (D-174);
  D-65's text (D-198); D-3/D-30 (D-168, D-203); D-42/D-88 path confinement (D-177); C-16 and C-19,
  additively (D-211, review S17). D-34 (no live network from agents or tests) and stab HG-1 (a live request
  to pathofexile.com or poe.ninja needs the user) are amended by D-297, as D-335 restates it, for
  poe.ninja's documented economy endpoints only: polite requests to them are allowed in development and in
  runs (C-12's currency rates), its builds, profiles and character API is never used, pathofexile.com and
  any request that carries a cookie or personal data stay gated, and tests stay offline. Stab HG-3
  (tool-set changes need the user) is resolved for the tools gap 23 names (D-173), for `guide_run` inside
  D-173's cap (cap lifted, D-316; D-216), and for C-49's additive price outputs and error prefix by D-202
  and D-218 once ratified. D-316 also resolved it for C-54's poe.ninja tools, which D-335 withdrew, so it
  now covers no new tool. The user's acceptance of amendments 4 to 7 resolved it for their changes to existing
  tools, each granted under HG-12 for its build only: D-360's path tools (M-15), `find_upgrades`' dated header and
  prefixed stops (D-372, M-16), the minion-skill changes (D-376…D-380, M-17) and a plain summon's figure (D-397,
  M-18). The user's go-ahead for the M-19 fix batch granted D-398's load warning, and the user confirmed the defect
  batch's changes (D-383, D-384, D-387, D-388) on 2026-10-18. No amendment adds a tool.

### 3.3 Since amendment 7 (amendments 8–14)

The historical text above stays as written; these facts have moved since:
- **Tool counts.** The count sites of §3.2.3 assert 85 tools, 84 with `POE_NINJA_DISABLED`, since WU-92 added
  `get_node_power` (C-55, D-417; amendment 9 §11; `tests/mcp/tradeQueryOptions.test.ts`:87–92), not 84 and 83; so
  §3.2.4's "No amendment adds a tool" holds only through amendment 8. Amendments 10 to 14 add no tool (M-27's gate: 85
  README rows, 78 core, 6 trade, 1 poe.ninja; WL:6433–6435).
- **Grants under HG-12** (§3.2.4's last sentences): each of amendments 8 to 14 granted its own build's tool and
  contract changes, for that build only; the list is in HG-12's row (§8.3).
- **Golden values** (§3.2.3) held at M-25's, M-26's and M-27's gates (AC-B5 among each gate's criteria; WL:5987–5995,
  :6146–6154, :6426–6441); the golden build's Life read 2,977 at M-27's fresh-clone check (WL:6435).


## 4. Slices and documents

### 4.1 The slice specs

Each slice spec carries its own functional requirements, acceptance criteria and work-unit blocks; this spine carries
what crosses them. When a slice and this spine disagree, the spine wins. No slice is folded past what the "Fold state"
column says: where it lists an amendment section, that section holds the slice's current text until the slice is
folded. The build-guide epic's changes to server contracts (§5.1) were not folded into the server slices, except the
in-place edits `03-trade.md`'s row lists, so each server slice's row names the rest. Line numbers (":n") are the slice
file's, at `6bb1a2a`.

### 4.2 Other documents a reader needs

- **The rationale record:** the server spine (`docs/stabilization/spec/README.md`) and the build-guide spine
  (`docs/build-guide/spec/README.md`), superseded by this one; the amendment files (§16); the server's vision, recon,
  gap analysis, review records (`docs/stabilization/review-spec.md`, `review-vision.md`) and spikes
  (`docs/stabilization/spikes/`, S-1…S-4; S-2 is the pin-upgrade procedure); the build-guide's change-vision and its
  review (`docs/design/2026-10-07-build-guide-workflow.md`, `…-workflow-review.md`), recon, gaps, spec review and
  spikes (`docs/build-guide/spikes/`: WU-30, WU-50, WU-51, WU-64).
- **The PoB gaps list,** `docs/build-guide/pob-gaps.md` (PG-1…PG-114): what the pinned PoB (v0.23.1, game 0.5.4) gets
  wrong or doesn't model, which verification labels links by, and the bridge's parse fixes cite (D-492). Since
  2026-11-06 it is also the user's record of PoB's bugs; PG-106…PG-108 came from an imported character (D-506),
  PG-109…PG-114 from PoB2's tracker the same day. Amendment 16's M-29 patched PG-106, PG-109 and PG-114 in the bridge
  (D-509), each entry's workaround now "corrected in the bridge"; PG-115 isn't recorded, since WU-154's check didn't
  hold (D-512).
- **The plan reference,** `docs/build-guide/plan-reference.md`: the plan schema and the compile record as built
  (schema 11 since M-29, its granted-quality rule), ahead of slice 02 (§4.1).
- **The bridge's patches,** `pob-api/API/ParseFixes.lua` and `pob-api/API/Patches.lua` (C-60), with
  `scripts/patch-fingerprints.mjs`; and the pin upgrade's script, `scripts/pin-upgrade.mjs` with S-2's probe
  `scripts/pob-internals.lua` (D-508), both since M-29.
- **The grounding fact sheets:** `docs/stabilization/grounding/` (`ctx-ts.md`, `ctx-lua.md`, `ctx-mcp.md`,
  `ctx-tooling.md`, `ctx-external.md`, with a `README.md`) for the server; `docs/design/grounding/` (the pin and
  spikes, the Claude Code platform, external game data) for the build-guide.
- **The data folders** (committed game facts; C-35, "C1 and E1 data files", is the JSON files' contract):
  `data/campaign/`, `data/enemies/`,
  `data/economy/prices.json`, `data/items/` (`sockets.json`, `item-only-skills.json`), `data/league/` (the league
  digest), `data/monsters/monster-mods.md`, `data/rules/` (the rule-changer index), `data/config-baseline.json`; and
  the skill's own reference data in `skills/build-guide/references/` (`toughness-targets.json`, `boss-floor.json`,
  `corruption.json`), with the pattern library in game knowledge's, `skills/game-knowledge/references/patterns.md`.
- **The plugin** `pob2` (C-57), the repository root since M-28: `skills/build-guide/` (`SKILL.md`, `references/`
  with the stage files under `stages/` and the shared rules in `runner-rules.md`, `scripts/`, `templates/`), the
  pipeline as built; `skills/game-knowledge/` (C-58, slice 04); the four agent definitions in `agents/`; the
  commands in `bin/`; `hooks/hooks.json`; the manifests in `.claude-plugin/`. §4.3's path line maps the old paths.
- **The work-logs:** `docs/stabilization/worklog.md` (the server's build, 2026-10-04 to 2026-10-06) and
  `docs/build-guide/worklog.md` (2026-10-08 to 2026-11-08); the as-built record that wins over an amendment's text.
- **The handoffs:** `docs/stabilization/build-handoff.md`, `docs/build-guide/build-handoff.md`,
  `docs/build-guide/build-handoff-amendments.md` (one section per milestone, M-14 to M-30, newest first, with the
  M-19 fix batch).

  ("design"): decision rows 1–49, the Target state, capabilities K1…O1 with their DAG and run order, the

  references;
- the gap analysis [`docs/build-guide/gaps.md`](../build-guide/gaps.md) ("gap n"), items 1–35. The user accepted every
  PROPOSED item and settled item 4 on 2026-10-07; item 32 is HG-14 here;

  [`docs/build-guide/spec-review-2026-10-07.md`](../build-guide/spec-review-2026-10-07.md) ("review Sn"), folded in as
  D-210…D-221 and C-50, and the coordinator's binding resolutions of the slice writers' open items (2026-10-07), folded
  in as D-222…D-238, A-31 and A-32;

  folded in as D-239…D-266 and D-305…D-314, and, from its section "2026-10-10: amendments 2 and 3, build start" on, as

  D-363…D-369 (M-15's build and WU-73), D-381 (2026-10-17), D-382 and D-383…D-388 (the Varashta run's blocker and defect
  batch), folded in v1.6;

  `2026-10-16-minion-skill-map.md`), and the Varashta run's feedback study and comparison
  (`2026-10-17-varashta-run-feedback.md`, `2026-10-17-varashta-comparison.md`). The comparison's remedies (its §7.2) are
  background, not requirements, except where amendment 7 adopts one;

  the pinned PoB gets wrong or doesn't model, which amendment 3 and C-53 cite;
- the resolutions of the fold's open points (2026-10-10): D-315…D-334, A-33 and HG-22.
- the amendments 8 to 14 and the user's rulings since v1.6, folded in this spine (§16, and §1 for the project state);
- amendment 15, approved and built 2026-11-06 (M-28), its rows as built (§16);
- amendment 16, approved 2026-11-08 (M-29, M-30), its rows entered as approved; M-29's as built since WU-158 (§16).

"worklog:n" means the same as "WL:n"; "stab WL:n" is a line of the server's work-log, `docs/stabilization/worklog.md`;
"a<N> §n" is amendment N's section n.

**Old paths to new** (amendment 15, D-499 and D-503; a15 §4, §8, §9), in effect since M-28 (built 2026-11-06). History
keeps the paths it was written with (a15 §15): `.claude/skills/build-guide/` → `skills/build-guide/`, except
`references/patterns.md`, `references/lookups.md`, `references/source-markers.md`, `scripts/rules-index.mjs`,
`scripts/league-digest.mjs` and `scripts/monster-mods.mjs`, which go to the same subfolders of `skills/game-knowledge/`;
`.claude/agents/build-guide/<name>.md` → `agents/<name>.md`, the type named `pob2:<name>` where a runner starts an agent
(bare in a run folder's text); `/build-guide` → `/pob2:build-guide`; the command lines `node
.claude/skills/build-guide/scripts/<x>.mjs` for `check-run`, `run-log`, `render-audit` and `render-notes` → the `bin/`
commands `pob2-check-run`, `pob2-run-log`, `pob2-render-audit` and `pob2-render-notes`; the run settings' compaction
hook → the plugin's `hooks/hooks.json`; `SKILL.md`'s hard rules → `skills/build-guide/references/runner-rules.md`,
except the legacy rule → `skills/game-knowledge/SKILL.md` (as built, WU-147).


## 5. Interface contracts

This section merges the two spines' contracts. §5.1 is the server's catalog, C-1 to C-21, from the stabilization spine's
§3. The build-guide spine's notes on those contracts (its §3.1, "Stabilization contracts this epic relies on or amends")
are folded into each row as an amendment, and its notes on server decisions and one requirement follow the table. §5.2
is the build-guide's own contracts, C-30 to C-54, from the build-guide spine's §3.2, with amendment 9's two new tools
added to its tool table: C-55 (`get_node_power`) and C-56 (`list_gems`' skill-type filter), and amendment 15's two
(built at M-28) after the path tools: C-57 (the plugin's layout and paths) and C-58 (game knowledge). Amendment 16's
eight follow: C-62 (`plan_from_build`) in the tool table, and C-59…C-61 and C-63…C-66 after C-58; C-59 (running
leagues) and C-60 (the bridge's patches) are built at M-29, the other six approved, not built. Amendments 8–15 appear
as **amended by** notes on the contracts they change, as the work-log records them built, and amendment 16's M-29 as
built notes, its M-30 as approved ones; §5.3 indexes them by amendment. The current tool count is in C-1: 85 tools, 84
with poe.ninja off, at M-27's gate (86 and 85 once M-30 is built, D-517).

### 5.1 The server's contracts (from the stabilization spine)

Status tags: **existing** (unchanged and relied on), **modified** (with the delta), **new**. The
"Defined in" column names the slice spec that carries the full detail. The summaries here are binding.

Here the slices are the server's (`docs/stabilization/spec/01-foundation.md`, `02-engine.md`, `03-trade.md`,
`04-advice-docs.md`). A note **amended by the build-guide epic** is the build-guide spine's §3.1 entry for that
contract, verbatim with its status tag, its section references renumbered.
