- **amended by D-503 (amendment 15, as built)**: the file moves to the game-knowledge piece,
  `skills/game-knowledge/references/patterns.md`, its text unchanged but for paths (a15 §8; C-58); built WU-145
  (`1fee40a`; AC-W120 compares it with its `a8d735c` text, paths normalized)

**New and modified tools.** Every new tool is group `core` (the poe.ninja tools of C-54 were withdrawn,
D-335); modified tools keep their groups. Inputs and outputs are at the level the seams need;
slice 1 or 2 carries argument types, wording and samples.

| ID | Tool | Status | Kind | Input | Output and compatibility |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| C-37 | `lookup_game_data` | new | read | `mode` (`gem`, `unique` or `mechanic`), `query`, mode filters (gem level and quality; unique slot), `max_results` (default 20, cap 30, D-225) | **gem:** PoB's tier (D-258); description; stat sets ("skill parts") with tooltip lines from PoB's stat describer; quality effects; weapon requirement; the level requirement per gem level and the attribute requirements (D-228); for supports, what they can and can't support. **unique:** matches by name, mod text or slot, each as item text ready for `add_item`. **mechanic:** keyword hits across gems, uniques, passives (ascendancy included) and mods, each with its kind and matching line. **No match (D-258):** "nothing matched", then the closest names of the kind searched, by edit distance to the query, then by name: in gem mode up to 5 active gems, then up to 3 supports (D-258a); otherwise up to 5; none for a query over 100 characters (slice 01 §2.2). **Unique mode, more than one unique** (D-239): compact entries (name, type, base, at most 5 matched lines, then `(and <n> more matching lines)`); the full text only for a single result. **Mechanic mode** takes an optional `kind` and its header counts each kind's hits (D-240). **Stat sets** show `Attack Damage: <n>% of base` and `Critical Hit Chance: <n.nn>%` (D-241). **Mod base types and essences** (WU-60, D-277; built): a mod hit is followed by `  Base types: …`, the bases whose spawn weight for it is non-zero, weighed only on the tables PoB rolls on a base (the item's own affix table, `Corruption`, and `Desecrated` mods with a desecrated tag); a mod of any other table gets `none (<the notRolled reason>)`. Essences are a fifth hit kind, `essence`, `[essence] <essence> (<item types>; <affix type>): <line>`, matched by its line or by the essence's name. **Lookup additions** (WU-53, D-299; built, merge `506d89e`): Gemling quality effects, "usable by", item-base implicits (a sixth kind, `base`), type-granting unlocks, what a support can ride, phrase searches with `…` gaps, a skill's hit-making children and stat sets on mechanic hits (slice 01 §2.2, AC-K58…AC-K65). **Minion skills** (WU-77, D-379; built): gem mode finds a minion skill by its exact name, one result per granted effect, after any gem of the same name: `Kind: minion skill`, a `Cast by: <minion> (summoned by <gem>)` line per summon, `Commanded: yes` for a command, a `Granted by: <passive> (<ascendancy>, node <id>)` line per passive that grants it, its cooldown, skill types and stat sets. **Legacy content** (D-384; built): legacy gems (the granted effect's `legacy = true`, not `Tier = 0`) and uniques PoB says are no longer obtainable are left out of every mode, gem lists included, and the ones a query matched are named on a `Left out:` line **amended by D-425 (amendment 9, as built)**: amendment 9 §24.2's C-37a to C-37d: gem mode prints each level's cost, Spirit reservation, cooldown and stored uses, and a support's cost and reservation multipliers (C-37a); mode `base` (as built kind `item-base`): bases of a `slot`, optionally up to a `level` and matching a `query`, with requirements, implicits, defences, weapon figures, Spirit and socket limit, hidden bases left out (C-37b); mode `config`: PoB's config options by key or label, with type, list values, show condition and tooltip line (C-37c); mechanic kind `constant`, `Data/Misc.lua`'s constants (C-37d); the description at 2,035 of 2,048 characters **amended by D-460 (amendment 12, as built)**: defect B2, a lookup without `kind` not returning a game constant's hit, didn't reproduce (a server built before the `constant` kind, likely); a test guards it, no code change (worklog:5909-5911) **amended by D-485, D-494 (amendment 14, as built)**: a mod hit's tiers show each tier's item level and range; the base mode prints `Sockets: <n> (PoB's ceiling <m> counts Exceptional and corruption)` from `data/items/sockets.json`; a generated unique's variants drop the item-only skills of `data/items/item-only-skills.json` (PG-105) |
| C-38 | `check_model_coverage` | new | read | one or more of `item_text`, `nodes` (ids or names), `gems`; or `build: true` for the loaded build | Per line, one status: fully parsed, partly parsed (with the remainder), unparsed, parsed to nothing (an empty mod list), possibly unread (a heuristic: spike S-b's by-name scan, D-226). A line is re-parsed alone, then with PoB's two-line join, before it is called unparsed (pin §B3). Per gem: skill stats possibly unmodelled (PoB's own red-tooltip rule, pin §B4), marked "check config" (D-123). Build mode also checks jewels in allocated tree sockets (D-242). The name scan also collects PoB's concatenation templates, so names PoB builds by `..` count as read (D-243). **Flags** (WU-54, D-300, D-336; built): a `flags[]` list beside the statuses, each `{flag, source, note, line?}`, changing no status and no count, listed in every mode: `mapped but unread`, `stat not mapped` (one per gem), `energy not modelled`, `level from support`, `granted skill undefined` and `support colour tie` (slice 01 §2.4) **amended by D-460 (amendment 12, as built)**: defect B5 fixed: coverage reads Hyrri's Ire, Obsessive Rituals and Ceremonial Ablution as read; no allowlist entry |
| C-39 | `manage_sets` | new | mutate | `set_type` (`item`, `skill`, `config`, `loadout`); `action` (`list`, `create`, `copy`, `rename`, `select`; loadouts list and select only); `id`, `title`; `use_second_weapon_set` for item sets | The sets of that type with ids, titles and the active one; for skill sets, the group at the build-wide main index; loadouts as `SyncLoadouts` lists them. Never calls `CopyLoadout`. A title's `{id}` list must match `[A-Za-z0-9,]` with no spaces. No delete (new ids are `#sets+1`, pin §C). A title holds at most one `{…}` tag, at its end, of non-empty ids; an untitled copy drops the tag (D-245). `list` runs as kind `read` (D-244) |
| C-40 | `set_tree_state` | new | mutate | On the active spec: `weapon_set_nodes` (allocated nodes tagged weapon set 1 or 2), `attribute_choices` (attribute node → strength, dexterity or intelligence) | The spec's tags and choices after the change, and the resulting attributes |
| C-41 | `compile_plan` | new | load (D-191) | `plan_path` (relative to the guides root); `build_name` (target in the builds folder, D-199; not used with `measure_only`); `stages` (optional subset, measure-only mode only, D-156; its semantics follow WU-30); `measure_only` (optional, D-208). Two modes, measure-only and final: run-folder creation and validation are `guide_run`'s (C-50, D-216) | Text summary: per stage the loadout, level, V1/V2 verdicts and ledger values, plus the paths written; the record itself is the file (C-33). The PoB file is saved in auto-level mode at the highest stage's level (D-138, D-149). The first stage reuses the new build's tree spec 1 and set 1 of each type, retitled, so the file holds one loadout per stage and nothing titled `Default` (D-215). The call replaces the engine's loaded build (D-191, D-210). It sends one engine request per stage plus a fixed overhead of five (D-221). **Measure-only mode:** compiles and measures in the engine as usual, then writes the compile record and the compiled XML beside the plan only; nothing goes to the builds folder or the write ledger. **Final mode:** an existing target is overwritten only under the write ledger's rule (D-211). **Errors** (`isError`): schema errors by JSON path (D-163); per-stage compile errors (unknown gem, item text that doesn't parse, unknown node, a node PoB's weapon-set connectivity rule leaves unconnected, broken weapon-set or attribute choice, an allocation PoB changed on import, D-213) with no PoB file written (D-162); an existing target with no write-ledger entry from this run (C-17's "already exists" wording), or one whose sha1 changed since this run's entry (C-17's changed-on-disk wording; D-211); a guides root overlapping the builds folder (D-220). Also (WU-55, D-293) a `compile_check` defect, a custom-modifier line PoB can't parse whole. **As built:** socket groups outside the named source are created with `includeInFullDPS` off (D-306, narrowed by D-337); off-hands are checked per stage, any dropped item is an error, and `loadoutWarnings` are recorded (D-310); config options PoB's save drops are restored in the written file (D-248); the text prints every V1/V2 finding up to 200 over all stages, then points to the record (D-273). `customMods` are written after the config options (WU-55, D-293, D-337; built), in a write of their own; every compile, measure-only included, creates the named source's groups (each stage's `rolledGroup`) with `includeInFullDPS` on and `groupCount` set to their `count`, so PoB computes Full DPS for them; every other group stays out (D-306, narrowed). S-N2 (WU-51) measured this at ×1.14 of the whole call. Since M-15 (built, WU-65…WU-67): the text gains a key-figure line per stage, so agents stop Grepping the record (D-356, amending D-273 (2)): DPS, life, energy shield, EHP, the four resistances and the V1/V2 finding counts, plus clear speed on endgame stages and the Arbiter verdict with its worst hit and ratio on `b` and `n`; the compiler writes an `items.anoint` as `{enchant}Allocates <notable>` on the amulet and `compile_check` refuses a notable without a recipe, an allocated one, or a stage without an amulet (D-358); it fills omitted reference hits and writes the record's `measures` (D-353, D-363); and V1 gains `tree-shape` and `support-sockets` (D-360, D-364, D-359). Since M-17 (WU-78; D-377, D-378, D-380): a group's `minionSkill` is resolved by name against the minion's live skill list at every compile, and the saved build carries PoB's own selection fields; `compile_check` refuses an unknown minion skill (listing the minion's skills), a name the list holds twice, a `minionStatSet` beyond the skill's stat sets, a group with no summon or two, a disabled summon gem, and a main group naming a minion skill whose `main.activeSkill` isn't the summon; the key-figure line names a minion source, `DPS <n> (<skill>, minion)`; V1 gains `not-granted`. Since D-382, every group number in the reply and the record is the plan's, PoB's own granted groups numbered after the plan's. Since D-383 and D-384, `compile_check` refuses a node whose unlock constraint is unmet (naming the node that unlocks it), a legacy gem and a unique no longer obtainable. Since WU-80 (D-397), a plain summon's group is read by its minion's figure, in the headline, the key-figure line (`DPS 78 (Skeletal Warrior Minion, minion)`) and the clear measure **amended by D-402, D-407 (amendment 8, as built)**: the compile swaps a "Tame Beast" gem for PoB's companion gem with the beast as its minion, sets `build.beastList`, writes kept modifiers by their rows and PoB's three companion options as earned state, and reads `data/monsters/monster-mods.md` only when a plan names a companion; skeleton compiles carry the measures (C-33); HG-12 granted for M-20 (amendment 8 §18) **amended by D-418, D-426, D-427, D-428 (amendment 9, as built)**: the compiler gives a `weaponSet` group its set's weapon slot (the main hand when its item grants no skill, else the off-hand when that grants none, else the main hand with V1's `weapon-skill-supports`); V1 checks Spirit on both sets and gains `empty-weapon-set`, `weapon-skill-supports`, `skill-slots` and, for a need without a resolving claim, `missing-reason`. The record, filed here by amendment 9 §24.2: on a stage that uses set 2, `outputsSet2` (the key figures with set 2 active, Spirit and its reservation among them) and `skillOutputsSet2` (each group's figures with set 2 active, keyed as `skillOutputs` is); on every stage the clear measure's `travel: {group, skill, usesPerSecond} \| null`, the first group other than the clear group whose active skill has the Travel type **amended by D-435 (amendment 10, as built)**: an item text with no `Implicits:` line gets its base's implicits (`Data/Bases/*.lua`'s `implicit`, ranges at 0.5); the key-figure line on variant stages (slice 02, `916ee94`); V1's `movement-tier` and the note `boots-exception` (D-440) **amended by D-451, D-452, D-455, D-460 (amendment 12, as built)**: V1's `curse-level-cap` and `custom-defence` on schema 8 and later, `tree-continuity` on schema 7 and earlier; a radius jewel lets a node in its radius pass the connection check, the jewels socketed before the tree (B3) (amendment 12 §3, §15) **amended by D-463, D-464, D-466, D-469, D-471 (amendment 13, as built)**: a draft mode, `{plan_path, draft_name}` (engine kind `none`), and `{plan_path, remove_draft}`, replying `Draft written: <relpath>` and `Draft removed: <relpath> (backup <path>)` (D-466); The Input cell's "Two modes" is stale: the modes are these, amendment 14's and the original two **amended by D-479, D-485, D-487, D-488, D-492, D-493 (amendment 14, as built)**: read-back (engine `none`, `readback.json`), adopt (`none`), notes (`none`, `notes_path`) and trade-links (`load`, `trade-links.json`) modes; **The path tools** (D-360, D-364; built, WU-66): the existing `read` tools `find_path_to_node` and
`get_nearby_nodes` (`src/services/treeService.ts`) never enter a class start, leave a start only while it holds
no tree, never step from the main tree onto an open start's exit or into an ascendancy node, and never path
through an unallocated alternate start (Path of the Sorceress, 12795), so they never open a second tree on one
start (slice 01 §2.11). They may stay conservative: they never route a loop back to a start's other exit, which
V1 accepts (D-364). Their handlers, arguments and output text are unchanged. That changes two contracts outside
C-37…C-50, which HG-12 reserves to the user. The user's acceptance of amendment 4, whose D-360 names both
tools, is that HG-12 decision (2026-10-11), so HG-12 lists D-360 among its exceptions and C-1 names it. No tool
is added, and the counts stay 84 and 83.

**amended by D-460 (amendment 12, as built)**: `find_path_to_node` paths to an untagged node through untagged nodes
only (B8); arguments and output unchanged. **amended by D-463 (amendment 13, as built)**: the path tools may leave a
start a socketed Split Personality opens. The counts are 85 and 84 since D-417 (C-1).

**C-57 The plugin's layout and paths** (new; approved 2026-11-06; built WU-145 (`1fee40a`), WU-146 (`aed1a63`), M-28;
slices 03 and 04; D-499, D-500, D-501; a15 §4–§6, Appendix A).
- **The plugin:** the repository root is the root of a Claude Code plugin named `pob2` (`.claude-plugin/plugin.json`,
  no `version` until amendment 18), which the repository lists as its own marketplace, `pob2-mcp`
  (`.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`, one plugin, `"source": "./"`).
- **Components,** in the default folders at the root: `skills/<piece>/` (`SKILL.md` with its `references/`,
  `scripts/` and `templates/`), `agents/` (the four agent definitions, flat), `hooks/hooks.json` (the compaction
  reminder, a `SessionStart` hook with the `compact` matcher) and `bin/` (`pob2-check-run`, `pob2-run-log`,
  `pob2-render-audit`, `pob2-render-notes`: POSIX shell, each running its script with Node from the plugin root).
  Everything else stays where it is and isn't a component.
- **Names:** a piece's command is `/pob2:<piece>`; a runner starts an agent as `pob2:build-guide-<x>`; text written
  into a run folder keeps the bare type name.
- **Paths:** a skill's or agent's body names a plugin file as `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/<root-relative path>`;
  references, stage references and templates name it root-relative; the task header carries `root`, the plugin root's
  absolute path; every command a runner runs from a skill file is a `bin/` command. Run-folder paths, PoB's own paths
  and plans' data-relative citations are unchanged.
- **The shared rules:** `skills/build-guide/references/runner-rules.md`, which every piece's runner reads whole before
  anything else and after a compaction (D-501).
- **Compat:** old run folders resume unchanged (the skill revision stays 14); the MCP server stays the user's own
  registration, its tools named `mcp__pob2__…`, until amendment 18 (D-506); the history keeps its paths (§4.3).
- **As built** (WL:6563–6566, :6583–6587, :6593–6595, :6605–6610): every plugin path in a skill or agent body carries
  `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/` (data and docs too), `<root>/` only in the task header's reference line and the verifier's
  prompt, a check enforcing it; the scripts' runner-facing messages name the `bin/` commands with their arguments
  (the game-knowledge builders' maintainer messages unchanged); a symbolic link to one `bin/` command fails, since `cd
  -P` resolves the folder: link the folder; the load check's `ICON_MISSING` advisory (no `icon` in `plugin.json`)
  doesn't fail it, the coordinator's call, Appendix A's manifest kept.
- **amended by D-523 (amendment 16, approved)**: `bin/` gains a fifth command, `pob2-league-digest`, which fetches the
  running leagues' digests on the user's side (a16 §19, C-66; target WU-166); the three new pieces follow this layout
  (`skills/compile/`, `skills/pricing/`, `skills/writer/`; C-63…C-65)

**C-58 Game knowledge** (new; approved 2026-11-06; built WU-145's file moves (`1fee40a`), WU-147 (`176366f`), M-28;
slice 04, `docs/build-guide/spec/04-game-knowledge.md`; D-503; a15 §8). The piece `/pob2:game-knowledge`: how to reason
about PoE2 builds, which a player or an agent asks in the session and other pieces read by path.
- **Its files:** `skills/game-knowledge/SKILL.md` (fewer than 150 lines); `references/patterns.md`, `lookups.md` and
  `source-markers.md`; the data builders `scripts/rules-index.mjs`, `league-digest.mjs` and `monster-mods.mjs`,
  maintenance commands run with `node` from the repository. `data/` and `docs/build-guide/pob-gaps.md` stay where they
  are.
- **Reads:** `runner-rules.md` first; its references; the committed data (the league digest, the rule-changer index,
  the monster table); PoB's gaps; the five lookup tools (`lookup_game_data`, `check_model_coverage`,
  `search_tree_nodes`, `list_gems`, `get_classes`); and on the web only poe2db.tw, at D-497's pace (the user's choice
  1).
- **Writes nothing.** It starts no agent, changes no engine build and prices nothing.
- **Answers** each claim with a source tag from `source-markers.md`, says once what it couldn't confirm, with its
  fallback, and holds that legacy content isn't in the game (D-395). Its description sends designing a build from a
  brief to `/pob2:build-guide`, and says no pob2 skill yet checks or improves a player's PoB file.
- The checks on its files stay in build-guide's checker until amendment 16.
- **As built** (WL:6659–6666, :6698, :6719–6725): `SKILL.md` 121 lines, frontmatter `name` and `description` only; the
  legacy rule under "Legacy means not in the game" there; without the lookups it says so once and answers from the
  committed files alone; the real cases passed at the first ask (AC-W125).
- **amended by D-507, D-515 (amendment 16, as built)**: it reads the running leagues' digests, from `leagues.mjs`
  (C-59), each head whole and the body by `grep`; an ended league's only when the user asks; poe2db.tw past a digest's
  fetch date or with none for the league asked about. Its runner searches with `grep` through Bash ("You need no Bash"
  goes) (a16 §3, §11); built WU-151 (`abbc81c`), WU-156 (`2acbd75`), M-29; as built: `SKILL.md` 125 lines; the league
  bullet's search is `grep` too, so AC-W142's no-Grep check covers it (WU-151's rebase round, WL:7103–7105); slice 04
  updated (WU-158)
- **amended by D-516, D-519 (amendment 16, approved)**: its description gains `/pob2:compile`, `/pob2:pricing` and
  `/pob2:writer`. The checker's rules on its files are tagged `game-knowledge` in the one checker (C-61) (a16 §12,
  §15; target WU-159, WU-162)

**C-59 Running leagues** (new; approved 2026-11-08; built WU-151 (`abbc81c`), M-29; WU-166's user-side folder
approved, not built, M-30; slices 03 and 04, `docs/build-guide/spec/04-game-knowledge.md` §2.3; D-507; a16 §3, §19,
Appendix A.1, A.2).
- **The dates:** a digest's line 3 carries, after the fetch date, `League: from <YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM> UTC until
  <YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM> UTC.`, or `… until no end announced.`, which `league-digest.mjs` reads from poe2db's homepage
  cards (a slot labelled "Running for" or "Starts in" is the start, "Ends in" the end, any other an event; a card
  belongs to the section whose version label it carries). In the same run it rewrites the sentence of every other
  digest in its output folder whose card the homepage shows; `--reflag` keeps the sentence.
- **The rule:** a league is running at `now`, the machine's clock as a UTC instant, when `now` is at or after its start
  and, if an end is recorded, before its end. `runningDigests(folders, now)` in
  `skills/game-knowledge/scripts/leagues.mjs` returns every running digest, the latest start first, then by name, a
  committed digest replacing a user-side one of the same league; as a command (`node
  <root>/skills/game-knowledge/scripts/leagues.mjs [--at <instant>]`) it prints the list. A digest with no `League:`
  sentence isn't running, and its reader names it once. Never a league not yet started; an ended one only when the user
  asks.
- **The folders:** `data/league/` and, from M-30, the server's league folder (C-66).
- **The readers, one rule:** the run log's `digest` is the list from skill revision 15 (a string or null before; each
  form where its revision puts it, and D-415's change line compares them); the runner's staleness line prints line 3
  of each running digest, newest first; the task header's `digests` carries their paths, which R1a, the researcher and
  `lookups.md` read; game knowledge takes the list from `leagues.mjs`. Trade's league for designs is unchanged (D-20).
- **As built** (WL:7082–7089, :7131–7149, :7165–7176): the committed digests' line 3 holds a16 §3's instants (Runes of
  Aldur from 2026-05-29 20:00 UTC, Forbidden Rites from 2026-09-04 20:00 UTC, both until 2026-12-11 20:00 UTC),
  checked by hand by the gate verifier; `init`, `resume` and the command print a `digests:` line, then each running
  digest's line 3 or the none line, then each undated digest once (no format in the spec); the test clock is
  `BUILD_GUIDE_TEST_NOW`, which every real-clock test sets; "the same league" is the same file name; a card with no
  start slot writes no sentence; the command's main guard compares real paths, so it runs through a symlinked root;
  the command, `node <root>/skills/game-knowledge/scripts/leagues.mjs`, is exempted from amendment 15's "every run
  command is a `bin/` command" check (AC-W115, `tests/static/amendment15Paths.test.ts`:112–117), a departure from a15's
  rule that a16 §3 and this contract force (the coordinator's call); the run-log and checker tests run on a layout
  holding only the two 0.5 digests, so a committed 1.0 digest can't move them; the tests' helper is
  `tests/helpers/runningDigests.ts`.

**C-60 The bridge's patches** (new; approved 2026-11-08; built WU-152 (`4130f96`), WU-153 (`b77d0f6`), WU-154
(`3865919`), M-29; the upgrade script's retirement, WU-155 (`6f4759e`, before its fix round); slices 01 and 02's
notes; D-509…D-512; a16 §5–§8, Appendix A.3).
- **Two modules:** `pob-api/API/ParseFixes.lua` keeps the data fixes (D-492); `pob-api/API/Patches.lua` holds the
  function patches, `M.PATCHES`, one entry per patch, `{pg, fingerprints, test, apply, revert}`. Each function patch is
  a wrap set on PoB's class table, `common.classes.<Class>`, so it reaches every instance and caller, and its `apply`
  refuses when the method it wraps is missing.
- **Bootstrap:** step 5c applies the data fixes, step 5d each function patch in its own `pcall`; a failure writes
  `[Patches] <pg> not applied: <error>` to stderr, and the engine starts without that patch, whose test then fails.
- **Fingerprints:** each entry, in both modules, lists the PoB code it relies on: a function (`{file, func, sha1}`), a
  data line (`{file, line, sha1}`) or a line range (`{file, from, to, sha1}`), hashed by `manifestSha1`. A static test,
  with no engine, fails naming the patch, the file and what changed.
- **Retirement:** each patch's test turns it off and shows the pin's own code still has the bug on a fixture; it fails
  once a pin fixes it. The upgrade script (D-508) retires a patch only when that "bug still present" case fails; a
  changed fingerprint alone, or any other failure, is judgment.
- **The patches:** PG-106's gem-group recount (D-510), PG-109's save and load (D-511), PG-114's granted quality from a
  wand, staff or sceptre (D-512). `vendor/` is never modified; an upstream PR only where PoB's `dev` lacks a fix, each
  approved by the user first (D-509).
- **As built** (WL:7093–7102, :7108–7130, :7150–7158): `Patches.lua`'s entries are `{pg, fingerprints, test = {file,
  case}, apply, revert}`, with `M.wrap`, which wraps only a method defined on the class itself and refuses a missing
  one, and `M.apply_all` for step 5d (`pob-api/bootstrap.lua`:85–92); the fingerprint reader and hasher are
  `scripts/patch-fingerprints.mjs` (an unimported `src/` module would fail `deadModules.test.ts`), each anchor unique
  in its file, matched after leading whitespace, the sha1 over CRLF-ended lines as `manifestSha1`; a block's `to` is
  the text that starts its last line; each "bug still present" case returns early when its setup failed, so a setup
  error fails only the case before it (`pg106.test.ts`, `parseFixes.test.ts`); PG-109 and PG-114 were laid out as
  stubs (`stub = true`, skipped by step 5d) until WU-153 and WU-154 built them; the switched mark is
  `Patches.SWITCHED = 'pob2Switched'`, a field PoB doesn't save, which WU-161 sets. The patches change the replies
  amendment 16's HG-12 grant lists for M-29 and nothing else.

**C-61 Piece runs** (new; approved 2026-11-08, not built: WU-159, M-30; slice 03; D-516; a16 §12, Appendix A.6).
- **The folder:** `guide_run {new_run: "<piece>"}`, with `compile`, `pricing` or `writer`, makes
  `Guides/<piece>/<date>-<n>/`; a piece's files sit at the folder's root (Appendix A.6's lists).
- **The run log:** schema 4 at skill revision 16, with `piece`, `mode`, `testConfig`, `noNinja`, `status` and `source`
  (`{kind: "builds-folder" | "import-code" | "run-folder", name?, sha1?, build?}`; an import code itself is never
  written), and no `slug`, `brief`, `dossier` or `builds`; the steps `read`, `measure`, `place` and `price`, the
  scopes `better` and `ladder`, and the id `file` where a pipeline run carries a card id.
- **A pricing stop ends the run:** status `stopped`, the rung asked for when none is placed, the lockout's end told;
  `/pob2:<piece> --resume <run folder>` after it is the retry, reusing every price the plan holds.
- **The checker:** one checker, each rule tagged by its piece (`run`, `game-knowledge`, `discovery`, `research`,
  `review`, `compile`, `writer`, `pricing`; a rule two pieces share carries both). A pipeline run is checked by every
  rule as today; a piece run by `run`'s rules as they apply, game knowledge's and its piece's. Rule names and messages
  don't change.
- **Each piece's `SKILL.md`:** fewer than 150 lines; frontmatter `name` and `description` only, at most 1,536
  characters, quoting the requests players make for it; its runner reads `runner-rules.md` whole first and after a
  compaction. A build the user names is matched against `list_builds`; the runner asks when none or several match. The
  compaction reminder names any pob2 run in progress.

**C-63 Compile, `/pob2:compile`** (new; approved 2026-11-08, not built: WU-161, WU-162, M-30; slice 05; D-519, D-521;
a16 §15, §17).
- **Its files:** `skills/compile/SKILL.md` and `references/stages/measure.md`. On a PoB file (a builds-folder name or
  an import code) it reads the file into a plan (C-62), measures it, shows the user what it measured and what seems
  wrong, and runs the better round on request.
- **The measure** (a coordinator step, D-130): the engagement distance; a measure-only compile with `measure_as:
  "non-budget"`, into the run folder only; the Spirit question when unreserved Spirit is below 0 on the measured set;
  the callouts (a16 §15's kinds), none changed without the user's word; the summary against `n`'s targets, shown as
  where the character is headed; the file card, `cards/file.md`, when a design round follows.
- **The better round:** D1-build, D1-tree, D1-finish and D1-gate on the read stage with `scope: "better"`, toward the
  pipeline's targets within the user's budget, a target the budget can't reach shown as a finding with its cost; the
  design gate as the pipeline's; the draft and final PoB files in the builds folder under their own names, never the
  user's file's. A character below the endgame (under level 65, or its owner says so) aims at `m`'s targets.
- It never writes the user's file, and prices only in `place` and the better round's D1-finish and D1-gate, announced
  as the pipeline's pricing is.

**C-64 Pricing, `/pob2:pricing`** (new; approved 2026-11-08, not built: WU-163, M-30; slice 06; D-520; a16 §16,
Appendix A.10).
- **Its files:** `skills/pricing/SKILL.md`, `references/pricing.md` (moved from build-guide, its text unchanged but for
  paths; the pipeline's five pricing steps read it there) and `references/stages/price.md`.
- **The league:** the file's `importLeague` when trade lists it; otherwise, or when it is Standard, hardcore, SSF or
  ended, the runner asks before any search.
- **The replace cost:** every filled slot and tree jewel, priced the cheapest way to the same must-have stats: an owned
  rare by its spec (its three or four lines ranking highest in PoB's weights for the slot, each at its tier's lowest
  roll); a unique by name; runes, soul cores, uncut gems and currency by poe.ninja's exchange; flasks and charms
  unpriced; a total by slot.
- **The place:** `m`, `b` or `n` by the replace cost against `pricing.md`'s targets (`b` only when no rare and no
  unique costs over about 5 divines); "in the campaign" below the endgame; without trade or under `--no-ninja` the user
  names the rung; never assumed.
- **Upgrades within the user's budget:** one `find_upgrades` call per gear slot with the price capped, each listing
  with PoB's gain, its price and link, in `upgrades.json`; the user picks. The notice before any trade request, the
  user's yes first above 30 searches; a stop ends the run (C-61).

**C-65 The writer, `/pob2:writer`** (new; approved 2026-11-08, not built: WU-165, M-30; slice 07; D-522; a16 §18).
- **Its file:** `skills/writer/SKILL.md`. It writes a guide for a build that passed F1, from a run folder the user
  names (a build-guide run's build, a file run's, or a compile run whose better round was accepted), into its own piece
  run folder, `Guides/writer/<date>-<n>/`.
- W1's task header carries `source`; `pob2-render-audit` and `pob2-render-notes` take `--source <folder>`; the checker
  checks its guide and audit against the source's files with the writer's rules.
- `pob2-run-log init` refuses a source with no D1-gate verdict (a measure-only compile run), naming the better round or
  a file run; a source keeping content a guide can't carry (D-395, D-465, D-484) is refused, pointing to a file run.

**C-66 The overlay** (new; approved 2026-11-08, not built: WU-166, M-30; D-523; a16 §19, Appendix A.11).
- **The sidecar:** `league-digest.mjs` writes `<slug>.overlay.json` beside every digest it writes, from the parsed page
  lines: socketables (name, `type`, `rank`, socket-bound, and per slot PoB's keys, lines and `statOrder`s) and uniques
  on bases the pin knows; `data/league/rune-slots.json` maps poe2db's slot labels to PoB's keys, checked against the
  pinned corpus by a test.
- **The league folder,** `<stateDir>/league/` (C-16), named in `guide_run {new_run}`'s reply. `pob2-league-digest
  --running --out <league folder>`, the fifth `bin/` command, runs at a pipeline or piece run's start, online and not
  under `--test`; when no running digest exists, or the newest records no end, it reads poe2db's homepage cards (at
  most once a UTC day) and fetches each running league's digest that is missing, at D-497's pace; it writes only the
  league folder.
- **The server** lists the sidecars of `data/league/` and the league folder in Node, a committed one replacing a
  user-side one of the same league, passes their paths to bootstrap step 5e, which adds each socketable and unique to
  PoB's data, and runs it again before a load or a compile when the set changed. An entry the pin already defines is
  skipped and logged.
- **Marks and limits:** a stage holding an overlay item has the record's `overlay`; the guide, the audit and every gate
  display say "measured with <name>'s lines from poe2db, not in PoB's release"; such a card or plan can be finalized
  with the mark. A line PoB doesn't parse stays unmeasured; an item on a base the pin lacks can't load; a new tree,
  skill or balance comes only with a PoB release and our pin upgrade (D-508).

## 6. Data and state model

Each spine's data model as it stands, with amendments 8–14 noted in place, then the entities those amendments added.

### 6.1 The server (from the stabilization spine)

| Entity | System of record | Lifetime | Written by | Concurrency rule |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| User build files | `POB_DIRECTORY` (PoB2 on Windows and this server both write) | durable | the user (PoB2); the server only via C-17 | Keys are canonical relative paths (D-52). Fingerprint check before every server write; a mismatch refuses the write |
| `LoadedBuild` record | TS memory (C-3) | server process | C-3 on load, new build, save of the engine's build, reload, restore | Mutated only under the engine mutex. Never null while an engine runs ("New build" when nothing was loaded) |
| Engine revision | TS memory (C-3) | server process | C-3: +1 per successful `mutate`/`load`/`lifecycle` call **and per auto-reload**; unchanged for `read` calls without auto-reload, and for failed calls | Under the mutex |
| Recovery snapshot | TS memory (C-3): the latest `{xml, revision, afterTool}` only | server process (D-44) | C-3 after each successful change and after every load; **discarded before every load** | Under the mutex |
| Generated-query cache | TS memory (C-14): LRU of 32, keyed `(revision, slot, normalized options)` | server process | WU-17 | Under the mutex (read and written inside `find_upgrades`) |
| Rate-limit state | TS memory (C-11), per policy | server process | every trade response | Single-threaded event loop. Several server processes reconcile only through the server's state headers (D-35)<br>**amended by D-422 (amendment 9) and D-461 (amendment 12), as built**: windows per policy and per kind (C-11); the pace's search sends, per server process, beside them |
| `/data/stats`, `/data/leagues` | TS memory (C-10) | stats: process lifetime; leagues: 4 h | lazily, when a trade call needs them | — |
| Currency rates | TS memory (C-12) | 60 min + ETag | lazily | —<br>**amended by D-387 (build-guide) and D-444 (amendment 11), as built**: also the exchange categories `get_item_price` prices from, each cached the same way per league and category (`src/trade/currency.ts`:14-16; C-12) |
| Build-parse cache | TS memory (`buildService`) | entry valid while the file's size and mtime are unchanged; LRU of 20 | read paths; invalidated by C-17 writes (D-58) | — |
| Server state files | state dir (C-16) | durable | snapshot, backup, export and preset tools | Snapshots and backups are written once, with timestamped names. Exports and presets are named by the caller, and are replaced only by an explicit overwrite or by re-saving the same preset |
| PoB checkout | `vendor/PathOfBuilding-PoE2` (gitignored) | until the pin changes | `mise run pob:fetch` only | Read-only at runtime |

**Migration:** none. The user's builds folder has no `.pob-mcp*` folders today (checked 2026-10-03). The
README notes that old locations are ignored.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| Guides root and run folders | `POB_GUIDES_DIRECTORY` (C-34) | durable, the user's | `guide_run` (creates each run folder, D-207, D-216); `compile_plan` (records, and measure-only XML, beside the plan); skill and agents (plan, dossier, guide, run log) | one run per folder; one coordinator calls compile; parallel agents write disjoint files<br>**amended by D-412, D-415 (amendment 9, as built)**: a run folder holds one build folder `builds/<id>/` per finalized card (C-34); a resume writes into its own earlier run's folder |

| `compile-record.json` | run folder | replaced by each compile of its plan (D-153) | `compile_plan` | under the engine lock; informational only, never an overwrite permission (D-211)<br>**amended by D-412 (amendment 9) and D-479 (amendment 14), as built**: in the build folder; a final compile also writes `final-build.xml` (`final-build-campaign.xml`) beside its record (§6.3) |
| Compiled PoB file | builds folder, through C-17 | durable | `compile_plan`, only in a run's final compile (D-208) | the write ledger's overwrite rule (D-211), then C-17's guard<br>**amended by D-413, D-415 (amendment 9), D-466 (amendment 13), D-479, D-487 (amendment 14), as built**: the final compile is F1's for an endgame-only build and the D2 gate's for a full one, and a campaign writes a file of its own beside the endgame one; files take the build's session name, cleaned (`<name>.xml`, `<name> (campaign).xml`), `first_free` on a first write; the notes write replaces `<Notes>` in the latest final file, a second ledgered write; an adopted file (the user's edit, taken on a yes) is replaced by the next write; drafts are files of their own (§6.3) |
| Write ledger `guide-writes.json` | state dir (C-16) | durable | `compile_plan`, final mode only, right after the PoB file (D-211) | under the engine lock; temp file then rename; one entry per builds-relative path; no agent can write it (D-177)<br>**amended by D-466 (amendment 13), D-479, D-487 (amendment 14), as built**: version 2: each entry records `kind` (`final` or `draft`) and `plan`, a version-1 entry reading as a final with no `plan`; a deleted draft's entry is dropped; a name is free when this build wrote it unchanged; entries gain `baseSha1` (the sha1 of the bytes a compile or draft wrote; an entry without it takes its `sha1`) and `adopted`; written also by the draft mode, its delete, adopt and the notes write, which updates `sha1`, never `baseSha1`; the format stays version 2 |
| C1 and E1 data files | repo `data/` | versioned with the repo | WU-39 | read-only at runtime<br>**amended by D-449 (amendment 12, as built)**: `data/enemies/arbiter-of-divinity.json` may carry `hits.<id>.evadable: false`, which makes that hit's evade chance 0; none does (amendment 12 §4). The data files added since amendment 7 are in §6.3 |

| Prices | `plan.json` `prices[]` | per run | coordinator; D1-build writes each `b` and `n` rare's entry, `query {slot, required, target}`, before it is priced (D-372) | no server cache (D-46, D-169); an emotion's exchange value is cached as the currency rates are (D-387). Prices stay as seen: the conversion to a price day is reporting only, so a re-price needs no migration (D-373)<br>**amended by D-444 (amendment 11), D-483, D-484 (amendment 14), as built**: every item the currency exchange sells is priced from its poe.ninja category, cached as the currency rates are (§6.1), and one poe.ninja doesn't list from the trade site by name (D-444); a `b` or `n` rare is a spec priced by the budget search and depth checks, never one listing (D-483); an entry records its `listing` and, on a `b` or `n` rare, its `spec`, both outside `buildSha1` (D-484) |

Amendments 10 to 14 keep schema 4 and add fields (C-36's notes), marked by `skillRevision` (11 since amendment 11, 14 today, `scripts/run-log.mjs`:257) with one boundary per revision in `skillRevisionFrom` |

**Migration:** none. Everything is new; the guides root is created by the first run, and the write
ledger by the first final compile.

## 7. Decision log

One table holds every decision, by ID: the server's D-1…D-92 (from the stabilization spine's §6),
the build-guide's D-100…D-399 (from the build-guide spine's §5), then D-400…D-495, the decisions of amendments 8–14, and D-496…D-498, the user's rulings at the fold,
then D-499…D-506, amendment 15's (approved 2026-11-06; D-499…D-505 built the same day, D-506 approved as the roadmap),
then D-507…D-525, amendment 16's (approved 2026-11-08; D-507…D-515 and D-525's M-29 part built at M-29, the rest
approved, not built).
The columns are the build-guide spine's. A server row's fourth cell, its rationale or rejected alternatives, follows
its decision text after *Rationale / rejected alternatives:*; a row without one had "—" there. §7.1 and §7.2 keep
each spine's own reading notes; §7.3 says what the fold of amendments 8–14, amendment 15's approval and build, and
amendment 16's approval and M-29 added.

### 7.1 The server (from the stabilization spine)

Status: **HUMAN** = decided by the user · **PROPOSED** = coordinator decision, recorded and overridable ·
**PROPOSED (build)** = a deviation a build unit recorded and its gate accepted, folded back from the work-log;
the as-built behaviour, overridable like PROPOSED.
Proceed on all of them; don't relitigate. Wording "(vision)" means the full text is in
[vision §3](../stabilization/vision.md); only the refinements are written out here.

### 7.2 The build-guide (from the build-guide spine)

**Numbering.** D-1nn lifts design row nn (D-101…D-149). D-151…D-185 lift gap items 1–35 (D-150 + n).
D-190…D-208 are the build-guide spine's own. D-210…D-221 are the adversarial review's fixes (review Sn), and
D-222…D-238 the coordinator's resolutions of the slice writers' open items, both 2026-10-07.
D-239…D-266 are the coordinator's and the user's decisions during the build, with their final wording
(work-log). D-267…D-275 lift amendment 1's items, D-276…D-284 amendment 2's, D-285…D-304 amendment 3's
(each row names its source). D-305…D-314 lift the work-log's unnumbered coordinator decisions.
D-315…D-334 resolve the fold's open points (2026-10-10). D-335…D-338 are the decisions of the amendments'
build, waves A and B (2026-10-10 and 2026-10-11), folded in v1.4. Folded in v1.5: D-339…D-344, the
decisions of the build's last units and WU-59 (2026-10-11); D-345…D-350, the user's decisions on cost and
scope after run A; D-351…D-361, amendment 4's proposals, which the user accepted on 2026-10-11 with its §1
answers; and D-362, the user's design gate. A row that amendment 4 supersedes or changes says "amended by
D-3nn (v1.5)" with the gist, and keeps its history. Folded in v1.6: D-363…D-369, the coordinator's decisions
during M-15's build and its follow-up WU-73 (2026-10-11 to 2026-10-14); D-370…D-375, amendment 5's (accepted
2026-10-16); D-376…D-380, amendment 6's (accepted 2026-10-16); D-381, the user's library cap, and D-382…D-388,
the coordinator's fixes after the Varashta baseline run (2026-10-17); and D-389…D-397, amendment 7's (approved
2026-10-18). A row that a v1.6 decision supersedes or changes says "amended by D-3nn (v1.6)". **Status:** *settled* = a ratified design
row or a user decision; *accepted* = a gap item the user accepted on 2026-10-07; *deferred* = a named gate
(HG-15, HG-19…HG-22); **PROPOSED** = the build-guide spine's, overridable at its ratification; *review fix* and
*resolution* = binding, overridable at ratification; **PROPOSED, pending the user** = a question put to
the user, proceeded on until answered; **PROPOSED by the coordinator, 2026-10-10** (or a later date) =
binding until the user overrides it. Each row also says *built*, or **target** (approved, not built, with
its work units), or *superseded by D-n* or *withdrawn → D-n* (kept so the id stays readable). Proceed on
all of them; don't relitigate. Rationale
for lifted rows is in the source row or the amendment.

(2026-11-04), each approved by the user and built: D-400…D-410, amendment 8's (finding the build's engine; M-20);
