D-449…D-462, amendment 12's (defence that holds, one tree per loadout; M-25); D-463…D-477, amendment 13's (a tree search
judged on targets, the finishing touches; M-26); D-478…D-495, amendment 14's (a PoB file at every gate, items as specs,
the tree tools' repairs; M-27); D-496…D-498, the user's rulings on the fold's questions (2026-11-04, WL:6470–6479).
Entered as approved at amendment 15's approval commit (2026-11-06): D-499…D-506, amendment 15's (the plugin layout,
the shared rules and game knowledge; M-28), of which WU-149 marked D-499…D-505 built the same day; D-506 stays approved
as the roadmap, which amendments 16 to 18 build. Entered as approved at amendment 16's approval commit (2026-11-08):
D-507…D-525, amendment 16's (the pieces that start from a PoB file, and launch day): D-507…D-515 M-29's, which WU-158
marked built (2026-11-09), D-516…D-524 M-30's, approved, not built, and D-525 the documents of both, its M-29 part
built. Each new row names its amendment section
("a8 §2", "amendment 10 §4", "a14 §4", "a15 §4", "a16 §3"); a bare "§n" in them is this spine's; in D-352…D-361 a bare
"(§n)" is amendment 4's section.

**Status of the new rows.** *approved* = the user approved the amendment (the date and the user's words where
recorded); *settled* = a user decision outside an amendment's text (D-410, D-496…D-498). Then *built*, with its work units,
milestone and commits where the work-log gives them, and *as built:* for every difference from the amendment's
text, with its work-log lines; the as-built record wins over the amendment (the v1.6 fold's rule). Until its build's
last unit marks it built, an approved row says **target** with its work units (amendment 15's rows, until WU-149;
amendment 16's, until WU-158 for M-29's rows, done 2026-11-09, and WU-167 for the rest). A row a milestone builds only
in part says *built* for that part and **target** for the rest (D-512's designers, D-515's probe, D-525's M-30
documents).

**Amended rows.** A row that a decision of amendments 8–16 changes keeps its text and gains, in its status cell and in
amendment order, `**amended by D-nnn (amendment N, as built)**: <gist>`; a section reference in the gist names its
document ("a12 §4"); a bare §n is this spine's. Server rows carry these notes too (D-22, D-47, D-49, D-64, D-70, D-71,
D-72, D-77, D-79), as do rows of amendments 8–13 that a later amendment changes. Amendment 15's notes read `**amended by
D-nnn (amendment 15, approved)**` until WU-149, which reworded them as built; amendment 16's read `**amended by D-nnn
(amendment 16, approved)**` until WU-158 (M-29's, reworded as built 2026-11-09) and WU-167 (the rest) reword them as
built; a note that named both milestones' decisions is split in two, the M-29 one as built. D-381's "superseded by
D-410" was already in place. A note "see D-nnn (a cross-reference added at the fold, not an amendment)" pairs a row with a later decision
that bears on it without naming it: D-461 with D-474, and D-428 with D-506 (added at amendment 15's approval). D-436 and
D-492 contradicted D-302, and D-445 contradicted D-393, without naming them; by the user's rule of 2026-11-04 (D-496)
those older rows are marked amended. At amendment 16's approval, by the same rule, D-384 and D-465 are marked amended by
D-519, whose owned-content exemptions contradict them without an Amends line naming them; D-428's cross-reference to
D-506 is now D-510's amendment, and D-504 gains a cross-reference to D-525 (the fold rule's middle step).

### 7.4 The decisions (D-1…D-525)

| ID | Decision | Status; divergence |
|---|---|---|
| D-1 | Work lands on the user's fork (`origin` = parkerault/pob2-mcp) | HUMAN |
| D-2 | Pin PoB2 `v0.23.1` (`7d6f530`) through `pob-pin.json` + sparse fetch into `vendor/` (vision). **Refined (D-31):** the HeadlessWrapper hash is CRLF-normalized. **Refined:** the readiness check compares the banner's `version.number` to the pin<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* A submodule downloads 1.55 GiB (S-2) | HUMAN / PROPOSED |
| D-3 | No constants of our own in advice (vision). **Refined by D-30** | PROPOSED; see §5.1 for the build-guide's note on it |
| D-4 | Delete 25 routed names plus their plumbing (vision). `find_item_upgrades` is also removed in WU-04 and replaced by `find_upgrades` in WU-17/WU-23 | PROPOSED; (e) HUMAN |
| D-5 | Trade generation and evaluation run in Lua through PoB's own classes; HTTP, limiter, auth, prefit, adjust loop, prices and URLs stay in TS (vision) | PROPOSED (S-1 PASS) |
| D-6 | `find_upgrades` replaces `find_item_upgrades`; inputs mirror PoB's dialog; actionable output (vision). **Refined by D-22, D-37, D-38, D-39, D-53, D-57** | HUMAN (use case) / PROPOSED |
| D-7 | Trade errors are actionable. The cookie goes only to search/fetch. **Refined by D-25** (User-Agent) and **D-24** (classification) | PROPOSED |
| D-8 | Engine concurrency and recovery: a mutex per tool call, request ids, timeout from the write, single-flight start, revision counter, snapshot after each change, E1/E4/E5/E6/E9 root fixes, env-gated test actions (vision). **Restated per vision D-8(d):** after a restart with no restorable snapshot, `read`/`mutate` calls return `isError` ("Engine restarted; the loaded build could not be restored. Load it again."), and `load`/`lifecycle` calls proceed. **Refined by D-59**<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* The SDK dispatches requests concurrently (`protocol.js:159-169`) | PROPOSED |
| D-9 | Writes never clobber the working build. File→file operations through the engine run snapshot → load → modify → save → reload under the lock (`withTemporaryBuild`) (vision). **Refined by C-17** | PROPOSED |
| D-10 | Runtime zod schemas for every TS↔Lua reply, validated in the bridge (vision). **Refined by D-26** | PROPOSED |
| D-11 | Currency: poe.ninja PoE2 is Divine-primary today. Budget authority = trade2's server filter plus a client post-filter on the normalized price. If poe.ninja is unavailable, use the server filter only and say so (vision). **Refined by D-33, D-54** | PROPOSED |
| D-12 | Golden fixture = the user's `Grenadator.xml`. **Done** in WU-01: `tests/fixtures/builds/grenadator.xml`, sha1 `e0c16f3…`, with `lastCharacterHash` blanked; it reproduces 109/109 `PlayerStat` pairs on the tag | HUMAN |
| D-13 | pnpm via corepack. **Refined by D-32** (corepack enabled by a mise postinstall) | HUMAN |
| D-14 | Trade and engine are both on by default. `POE_TRADE_DISABLED=true` opts out of trade. `POB_LUA_ENABLED` and `POE_TRADE_ENABLED` are removed. No network I/O before the MCP handshake | HUMAN / PROPOSED |
| D-15 | Weighted search needs a session: `POESESSID` in the gitignored `.env` (the user accepts a cookie on disk), with a trade-site link as the fallback. **Mechanism refined by C-9, D-24, D-49**<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* GGG OAuth registration is closed; `account:trade` is a PoB-only grant | HUMAN / PROPOSED |
| D-16 | Setup and registration (vision). **Refined:** the registration command is exactly `claude mcp add pob2 -s user -e "POB_DIRECTORY=/mnt/c/Users/parke/Documents/Path of Building (PoE2)/Builds" -- mise exec -C /home/parker/projects/pob2-mcp -- node build/index.js`, after `mise trust`. Verified 2026-10-03: Claude Code 2.1.289 lists the tools and completes a call against the current SDK-1.20.2 server. **Amended 2026-10-18 (the user):** the repository is public, so the README shows only the generic form, with `<your PoB2 Builds folder>` and `<path to this repo>` in place of the paths | PROPOSED; POB_DIRECTORY HUMAN (Q6) |
| D-17 | Local gate `mise run test`. Engine tests fail rather than skip unless `POB_SKIP_ENGINE=1`. In-process MCP tests. Worktree bootstrap `mise trust && pnpm install --frozen-lockfile && mise run pob:fetch`. Coverage is reported, not gated (vision). **Refined by D-32** | PROPOSED |
| D-18 | **Server state lives in `${XDG_STATE_HOME:-~/.local/state}/pob2-mcp/`** (snapshots, backups, exports, presets), never in the builds folder. PoB2's build browser lists every subfolder and `.xml` there (`BuildListHelpers.lua:22-76`)<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* Keeping `.pob-mcp/` in Builds would make snapshots show up in PoB2 as builds | **HUMAN** (2026-10-03) |
| D-19 | **Disk-change policy.** At the start of each engine call, if the loaded file changed on disk (fingerprint):<br>• when the engine is clean (no change since the last load/save), auto-reload it and say so;<br>• when dirty, don't reload, warn, and require an explicit reload or a save under a new name.<br>Saving over a changed file is always refused (C-17)<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* Warn-only was rejected for a collaborative workflow | **HUMAN** (2026-10-03) |
| D-20 | **Default league:** `POE_LEAGUE` if set; else the first `/data/leagues` entry (API order) that isn't `Standard` or `Hardcore` and doesn't start with `HC `. That is "Forbidden Rites" in the fixture, and it is also the golden build's `lastLeague` | **HUMAN** (rule) / PROPOSED (HC exclusion) |
| D-21 | Alpha = the trade vertical, plus the build-file writer that makes it safe to use against the user's real folder (§9) | PROPOSED |
| D-22 | **`find_upgrades` bounds:**<br>• ≤ 5 searches per call (PoB `maxRecursion`, `TradeQueryRequests.lua:111`);<br>• a **90 s deadline from tool-call arrival**, which includes waiting for the engine lock;<br>• every HTTP and engine step's timeout is clipped to the time remaining, and no step starts with < 1 s left;<br>• on `would_wait` (a limiter wait > 30 s or past the deadline), `locked_out`, `rate_limited` or a cut step, stop and return what has been evaluated, plus the link and the reason;<br>• `limit` defaults to 10, at most 20;<br>• progress notifications when a `progressToken` is present;<br>• the caller's `signal` stops it and releases the lock.<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* Claude Code backgrounds MCP calls after 120 s (`CLAUDE_CODE_MCP_AUTO_BACKGROUND_MS`) | PROPOSED; **amended by D-461 (amendment 12, as built)**: searches go at least 45 s apart, at most 7 in any 300 s (`TRADE_SEARCH_PACE`, `src/trade/rateLimiter.ts`:176); `would_wait` is a limiter wait over 60 s (`DEFAULT_MAX_WAIT_MS`, `rateLimiter.ts`:147), not 30 s; under the pace the 90 s deadline fits at most two of its ≤ 5 searches (amendment 12 §16; implied there, not in its Amends list) |
| D-23 | **Complexity prefit:** before sending, estimate complexity with PoB dev head's budget model: `54 (weight group) + 4 (category+rarity) + 4 (and group) + 4 per and/not/weighted filter + 3 per each of mirrored:false, price, max level, sockets + 2 per extra query key`. Drop the lowest-weight weighted filters until `200 − estimate ≥ 4`. If a 400 `too_complex` still comes back, drop the lowest-weight 25 % (at least 1) and retry **once**, then fall back to the link with the server message<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* Every 4xx counts toward GGG's invalid-request threshold. The model is conservative on all 5 fixture data points (A-3) | PROPOSED |
| D-24 | **Session detection uses the rate-limit header**, and applies **only to requests whose body has a `type:"weight"` stat group** (the classifier knows its own body; it never parses the message). For such a request with a cookie sent, a 400 code 2 means `too_complex` if `Account` is in `X-Rate-Limit-Rules` (case-insensitive), and `session_rejected` otherwise (A-13). Any other 400 code 2 → `invalid_query` (`isError` with the server message). 401/403 → `auth`, a generic "trade rejected the request (HTTP n)" plus the refresh hint<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* GGG documents code 2 as "Invalid query" and says "use the code rather than the message" | PROPOSED |
| D-25 | **User-Agent** = `<package.json name>/<package.json version>` (today `pob2-mcp-server/0.2.2`), with no contact, for trade and poe.ninja. Defined once in `src/utils/userAgent.ts`. `POE_TRADE_USER_AGENT` is removed.<br>• **Exception:** share-site fetches (pobb.in, pastebin) send `Mozilla/5.0 (compatible; <that constant>)`, as the inherited code does, because bot filters block plain UAs (A-15).<br>• **Recorded deviation:** poe.ninja asks for a contact and for desktop apps to proxy; we do neither, and D-11 is the fallback if it blocks us<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* GGG's `OAuth {clientId}/…` format requires a registered app (closed). A contact would identify the user | PROPOSED |
| D-26 | `zod@^3.25.76`: the v3 API, deduplicated with the SDK's copy. Never pass zod schemas to SDK APIs. `zod-to-json-schema@3.24.6` is a devDependency, for the schema-agreement test (C-14)<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* SDK 1.20.2 silently empties v4 schemas (probe) | PROPOSED |
| D-27 | **Error surface.** Every error a tool handler produces → `isError: true` with an actionable text, set centrally in the router. Unknown tool → JSON-RPC −32602. Invalid arguments for tools with zod input schemas (`find_upgrades`, `trade_query_options`) → `isError` naming the field<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* MCP 2026-07-28 moves input-validation errors into `isError` so the model can self-correct | PROPOSED |
| D-28 | The engine child env is `deps.env` minus `POESESSID`/`POE_SESSION_ID`. The server exits on stdin `end`/`close`, killing luajit. Startup errors include the cause (`luajit` not on PATH; the last stderr lines)<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* The SDK's stdio transport ignores EOF (`stdio.js:27-34`) | PROPOSED |
| D-29 | `main.disableDevAutoSave = true` at bridge boot. `load_build_xml` pre-validates (non-empty, parses, root `PathOfBuilding2`) and post-checks (`main.mode=="BUILD"`, `newMode==nil`, `buildName==name`); failure → `LOAD_FAILED`. The readiness probe is the banner plus `ping` (no XML load); WU-06 changes it in the same merge as the Lua side<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* Any failed load after a good one wedges the engine through dev autosave (ctx-lua X3) | PROPOSED |
| D-30 | **Advice judgments** come only from: (a) PoB's warning lines, obtained by a new `get_warnings` action that runs `build:RefreshStatList()` with `characterLevel` and `characterLevelAutoMode` restored afterwards; (b) `Missing<Elem>Resist > 0`; (c) per type, `<Type>MaximumHitTaken < <Type>EnemyDamage` for the configured enemy<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* `totalEnemyDamage` sums 5 types, while `<Type>MaximumHitTaken` covers one (ctx-lua X5) | PROPOSED; see §5.1 for the build-guide's note on it |
| D-31 | `pob-pin.json.headlessWrapperSha1` = `77a3e3a9b7387ebc57ef0a35bb17c382282e3c7c` (CRLF-normalized, the manifest scheme)<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* S-2's `784d675…` is the raw-LF hash | PROPOSED |
| D-32 | **Toolchain:**<br>• `node = { version = "24", postinstall = "corepack enable" }`;<br>• pin the LuaJIT asdf plugin via `[plugins]` to the **full** SHA `bd4977ef2c2cfd0659a4bb39acb37bd5104fb7fc`, verified in a scratch `MISE_DATA_DIR` (else record the limitation as an A-n and drop the pin);<br>• move `LUAROCKS_VERSION` to `install_env`;<br>• mise tasks contain **no pipes** (the task shell lacks pipefail);<br>• delete `coverageThreshold` from `jest.config.cjs`;<br>• remove `@types/chokidar` and `@types/node-fetch`;<br>• keep `@types/node` at ^20 (it already declares `util.parseEnv`).<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* A fresh Node 24 has no pnpm without corepack enabled. mise rejects abbreviated plugin SHAs | PROPOSED |
| D-33 | **Currency:** read `core.primary` from every poe.ninja response (never hard-code divine). Join listings on `listing.price.currency` = poe.ninja ids. Display normalized prices in the budget currency (in exalted when the budget is "Exalted Orb Equivalent"). Cache 60 min with `If-None-Match`<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* poe.ninja docs: per-response primary; "ETag-based. Use conditional requests" | PROPOSED |
| D-34 | **No live network from build agents or tests.** All tests use fixtures. Jest makes global `fetch` throw, and spawned servers get an explicit safe env (C-18). Only the coordinator (fixture capture, with user approval) and the user (WU-20/21) make live requests to pathofexile.com or poe.ninja<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* Rate limits are per IP and account; an agent run could lock the user out | PROPOSED; **amended by D-297 (amendment 3, as D-335 restates it)**: polite requests to poe.ninja's documented economy endpoints (serial requests, back-off on HTTP 429, nothing fetched twice that a cache could hold) are allowed in development and in runs; its builds, profiles and character API is never used; pathofexile.com and any request that carries a cookie or personal data stay gated; tests stay offline (§3.2.4) |
| D-35 | Several server processes (several Claude sessions) coordinate only through the server's limit-state headers, which count every client on the IP or account. The limiter adopts server state that is higher than its own. A new process may take one 429 during another process's active lockout; it then honours `Retry-After`<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* Persisting lockouts across processes was considered and rejected: it saves at most one 429 per process start | PROPOSED |
| D-36 | No `structuredContent`/`outputSchema`. Results stay text<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* Agent SDK docs: text blocks are dropped when `structuredContent` is set, which would hide warnings | PROPOSED |
| D-37 | **`find_upgrades` defaults mirror PoB's dialog:**<br>• sort weights = the build's saved `<TradeSearchWeights>`, else FullDPS 1.0 + TotalEHP 0.5;<br>• `include_mirrored` true;<br>• `weight_corrupted_mods` true unless the slot's current item is corrupted;<br>• status `securable` (Instant buyout);<br>• `fetch_pages` 2;<br>• rune and anoint behaviour `copy`;<br>• `rank_by` `stat_value`;<br>• currency = Exalted Orb Equivalent.<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* ctx-lua X7, X8 | PROPOSED |
| D-38 | `find_upgrades` slots = what PoB's generator supports: gear, weapons and their swaps, rings, amulet, belt, flasks, gear jewel sockets except weapon ones, and tree jewel sockets. Jewel slots require `jewel_type`. Charm, Arm and Leg slots and weapon jewel sockets error with the supported list<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* `TradeHelpers.lua:256-302` | PROPOSED |
| D-39 | Both trade links use `https://www.pathofexile.com/trade2/search/poe2/<league>?q=<urlencoded query JSON>`. The league is encoded as PoB's `buildUrl` effectively does: every byte outside `[A-Za-z0-9-._~]` → `%XX`, **so a space is `%20`** (PoB's trailing `gsub(" ","+")` never matches). That equals `encodeURIComponent` for league names. The exact-item link applies PoB's recipe (weight `[floor(w,1)−1, round(w,1)+1]` plus `trade_filters.filters.account.input`)<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* PoB's tag omits `poe2` in the exact-item URL (unverified on the site); the realm form is the one PoB uses for no-session links. WU-20 verifies, and may also test the shorter offline search-id form | PROPOSED |
| D-40 | No automatic retry on network errors or 5xx (except D-23's single complexity retry)<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* Retries count against limits | PROPOSED |
| D-41 | The README carries GGG's third-party notice verbatim<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* GGG developer guidelines | PROPOSED |
| D-42 | `export_build`'s `output_directory` must resolve inside `POB_DIRECTORY` or the state dir. **Refined by D-81, D-88** (never inside the state dir's `backups/` or `snapshots/`)<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* Every path argument is confined | PROPOSED; see §5.1 for the build-guide's note on it |
| D-43 | Watcher: polling (`interval: 1000`) when the watched path starts with `/mnt/`; `.xml` only; depth unlimited; cache invalidation by canonical relative path<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* inotify doesn't see Windows writes on drvfs (WSL #4739) | PROPOSED |
| D-44 | The recovery snapshot lives in memory only, latest one kept<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* Files on disk are the system of record | PROPOSED |
| D-45 | **The tool-hygiene test (AC-F26) uses a strict allowlist**, `tests/fixtures/tool-hygiene-allowlist.json`, with entries `{tool, check, owner}`.<br>• An allowlisted check that passes fails the test.<br>• **Any** unit may remove an entry its change makes pass, recording the actual fixer in the work-log.<br>• A unit may add an entry only with an owner and a reason, and only where this spec says so (WU-08's interim guard, D-51).<br>• Every tool has ≥ 1 `expect:"ok"` sample; error samples are additional, each with a reason.<br>• Done requires an empty allowlist.<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* Lets M-1 land before the fix units, without hiding regressions or deadlocking an early fixer | PROPOSED |
| D-46 | Trade search and fetch responses are never cached. Only `/data/*` and poe.ninja are<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* Listings go stale; D-6 forbids caching listings | PROPOSED; see §5.1 for the build-guide's note on it |
| D-47 | Engine request timeouts: 30 s default (`POB_TIMEOUT_MS`), 60 s for `trade_generate_query`. Inside `find_upgrades` they are clipped by D-22<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* S-1 measured generation at ≤ 4.4 s; headroom for slow boxes | PROPOSED; **amended by D-417 (amendment 9, as built)**: `node_power` is a long action at 120 s (amendment 9 §11); **amended by D-474 (amendment 13, as built)**: `compile_stage` joins the long actions at 120 s, as `node_power` (amendment 13 §15) |
| D-48 | Commits by build agents in worktrees use `-c commit.gpgsign=false`. The orchestrator sign-rebases once at the end with the user present<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* 1Password signing pops a Windows approval that headless agents can't answer | PROPOSED |
| D-49 | **`find_upgrades` fallbacks are results** (`isError: false`) that start with `Trade search not run: <reason>` and carry the trade-site link; since build-guide D-494 (amendment 14) the text starts with its cause, `[trade:no_session]`, `[trade:session_rejected]` or `[trade:too_complex]`, then `Trade search not run: <reason>`.<br>• **The fallback cases:** no session, an `op://` or malformed cookie, `session_rejected`, and a second `too_complex`.<br>• **No network before a no-session fallback.** It is decided right after query generation, **before** the stat-data or league fetches, whenever the league comes from the input or `POE_LEAGUE`.<br>• **Partial results.** An `http`/`network`/`timeout` error after ≥ 1 listing was evaluated returns those listings with a "Stopped early" note.<br>• **Genuine failures** are `isError`: invalid input, engine errors, and errors before any evaluation.<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* The link is the designed outcome of D-15; flagging it as a failure would push Claude to retry instead of handing the user the link | PROPOSED; **amended by D-494 (amendment 14, as built)**: the fallback's cause prefix (already folded into this row's text, `b31d5df`) |
| D-50 | **Tuning constants.** These are coordinator choices, listed here so they can be changed in one place: <br>• engine: banner wait 15 s, stderr ring 50 lines (20 shown), quit grace 2 s;<br>• Jest: `testTimeout` 60 s;<br>• trade: error-message truncation 300 chars, `Retry-After` default 60 s, query-cache LRU 32, `max_level` 1–100, `min_empty_rune_sockets` 1–6, `sort_stats` 1–10, `report_stats` ≤ 10, choice lists ≤ 200;<br>• `add_gem` suggestions ≤ 5;<br>• `import_build` inflate cap 16 MiB (unchanged from today);<br>• fetch timeout 120 s (AC-F2). | PROPOSED |
| D-51 | **User-visible behaviour changes** this spec makes beyond the vision §4 rows (the user can veto any at ratification):<br>• (a) `analyze_defenses`' `build_name` becomes optional; the default is the loaded build.<br>• (b) `restore_snapshot` always backs up; `backup_current` is still accepted but ignored.<br>• (c) `set_build_notes` goes through C-17 (backup; refused over a changed loaded file).<br>• (d) A save under a new name re-points the loaded build to it, as PoB's Save As does.<br>• (e) `lua_stop` names any unsaved changes it discards.<br>• (f) Between WU-08 and WU-13/WU-18, `analyze_build`, `compare_builds`, `validate_build {build_name}` and `analyze_defenses {build_name}` return `isError` instead of silently replacing the engine's build.<br>• (g) The five `*TakenHit` sort stats aren't offered (D-57).<br>• (h) `search_trade_items` drops `min_links` (PoE2 has no links) and gains `min_rune_sockets`. Its handler adopts the schema's existing argument names. | PROPOSED |
| D-52 | **Canonical build paths.** On case-insensitive filesystems (drvfs), every build path used as a key, a display name or a write target is canonicalized to its on-disk casing by `canonicalBuildPath(name, baseDir)` in `src/utils/pathSanitizer.ts`:<br>• each segment takes the exact `readdir` match, else the single case-insensitive match;<br>• a new final segment keeps the caller's spelling;<br>• several case-insensitive matches and no exact one → `isError` "ambiguous build name".<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* `/mnt/c` resolves any casing to one file, and `fs.realpath` keeps the caller's casing (verified 2026-10-03) | PROPOSED |
| D-53 | **`find_upgrades` output budget:** listings render in rank order in full (stats, `Buy:` line, item text) while the output stays ≤ 30,000 characters. The rest render as one-line entries (name, price, score, `is_upgrade`, seller) with a note<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* One exact-item link is ~4,000 chars (the whole query, URL-encoded); 10 full listings ≈ 48k chars, past Claude Code's 10k-token warning | PROPOSED |
| D-54 | **Partial currency conversion mirrors PoB:** if any listing left after the budget filter has no convertible price, the `price` and `stat_value_per_price` modes fall back to `stat_value`, with a note. Unconvertible listings are never counted over budget (the server filter applied, A-10), and they are flagged<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* `TradeQuery.lua:863-868, 919-930` | PROPOSED |
| D-55 | **Canonical mod order.** `Trade.lua` sorts `gen.modWeights` by (`meanStatDiff` desc, `\|weight\|` desc, `tradeId` asc) before `FinishQuery`, and `rankedWeights` uses the same order. `requiredModChoices` are sorted by `tradeId`<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* PoB sorts with an unstable comparator over `pairs`-ordered input, and the pinned LuaJIT randomizes string hashes per process | PROPOSED |
| D-56 | **Fetch-result sanitizing.** TS drops `null` fetch entries and listings without `price` before sending entries to Lua, and counts them. `Trade.lua` evaluates each item in `pcall` and reports failures in `rejected`<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* PoB reads `listing.price.amount` unguarded (`TradeQueryRequests.lua:437`); one bad entry fails a 10-item block | PROPOSED |
| D-57 | **Baseline and Taken stats.**<br>• `baselineScore` = `WeightedRatioOutputs(baseOutput, ReduceOutput(baseOutput), weights)`, computed in Lua. That is the score PoB's own item path gives the unchanged build, so the equipped item scores exactly the baseline.<br>• The five `*TakenHit` stats are excluded from `sort_stats`/`sortStats`, because PoB's item path applies their transform twice and ranks them backwards.<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* Probe on the golden build: Σ weights is wrong for base-0 stats (FullDPS 1 + BlockChance 0.5 scores 1.0, not 1.5) | PROPOSED |
| D-58 | The build-parse cache keys by canonical relative path, and validates each hit by `{size, mtimeMs}` (no TTL; LRU of 20). C-17 writes invalidate the written path<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* The 60 s name-keyed cache served stale builds after saves | PROPOSED |
| D-59 | **Only the session starts the engine.** `request()` on a dead client rejects with `EngineExitedError`; it never spawns. If the engine dies during a tool call (pid recorded before `fn`), the call fails and step 6 is skipped<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* A mid-call self-restart produced a blank engine labelled as the user's build | PROPOSED |
| D-60 | `lua_save_build` = `export_build_xml` + the C-17 writer. The Lua `save_build` action, its schema and `PoBLuaApiClient.saveBuild` are deleted (WU-22)<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* C-17 must see every write | PROPOSED |
| D-61 | **`search_trade_items` loses its value scoring and gains normalized prices** (an amendment made during the build, 2026-10-04).<br>• WU-04's list said `costBenefitAnalyzer.ts` would become unreferenced, but `search_trade_items`' `formatSearchResults` still ranked and annotated results with it: a value score, a tier, a best-value rank and per-chaos stat lines.<br>• That score is build-blind (fixed weights, the same for every character), and `find_upgrades` is the build-aware ranking. So WU-04 removes the scoring and deletes the file, as listed. Listings keep the order the trade site returned.<br>• WU-15 shows each listing's native price plus its primary-currency equivalent from C-12 `convert` (AC-T46).<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* The currency conversion was the only part worth keeping | HUMAN (2026-10-04) |
| D-62 | **The canonical export is compared order-insensitively across a temporary reload** (WU-13; AC-E16, AC-E48, AC-E51, AC-E56 amended).<br>• After `withTemporaryBuild` (D-9) puts the working build back, the pinned PoB2 re-serializes `pairs()`-ordered tables in a new order (`vendor/PathOfBuilding-PoE2/src/Classes/PassiveSpec.lua:226-311`): the `<Spec>` node list and the tree `<URL>` that encodes it, `<AttributeOverride>` node lists, `<Input>`/`<Placeholder>` element order, and attribute order. A literal "identical" can't hold.<br>• **Rule** (`tests/helpers/reloadCanonical.ts:61-70`, `canonicalizeAcrossReload`): on top of the strict canonical export, sort every tag's attributes by name; sort the node-id sets (`nodes` on `<Spec>`/`<WeaponSetN>`, `strNodes`/`dexNodes`/`intNodes`) numerically; sort `masteryEffects` entries; drop the `<URL>` body; sort each run of consecutive `<Input>`/`<Placeholder>`, `<Socket>` and `<WeaponSetN>` elements. Everything else is compared as exported.<br>• It applies only to samples whose tool reloads the working build (`analyze_build`, `compare_builds`, `export_build` with notes; `tests/integration/engineKinds.test.ts:107-112`). Every other check keeps the strict comparison, so a read tool that reloads behind the session's back is still caught. A unit test shows real changes are still detected (`tests/integration/buildFiles.test.ts:103-156`).<br>• AC-E56's interim "`compare_builds` returns `isError` until WU-13" is superseded: since WU-13 it succeeds and leaves the state unchanged (`tests/integration/engineSession.test.ts:224-237`).<br>• The `lua_set_tree` hygiene sample re-applies the build's own nodes and declares `stateChange:false` (`tests/fixtures/tool-calls.json:64`)<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* Gates accepted it; rejected: a strict compare (fails on every reload), or comparing only stats (misses real tree/config changes) | PROPOSED (build) |
| D-63 | **Bridge request ids continue after the readiness ping** (WU-07; AC-E7 amended). `start()` sends `ping` as id 1, so the first three `request()` calls after it get ids 2, 3 and 4 (`tests/unit/pobLuaBridge.test.ts:79-110`)<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* AC-E7's "ids 1, 2 and 3" contradicted the C-4 readiness ping (AC-E23) | PROPOSED (build) |
| D-64 | **Listed-tool counts follow the final surface** (WU-17, WU-23, WU-19; AC-T41 and AC-G4 amended).<br>• AC-T41 asserts 76 (75 + `find_upgrades`), because WU-23 has landed when it runs (`tests/mcp/tradeQueryOptions.test.ts:83-89`).<br>• `POE_NINJA_DISABLED=true` hides `get_currency_rates` (C-1 tool groups), so a server with that flag lists 75. `fresh-clone-check.mjs` lists 76 on a listing-only server without the flag, and asserts 75 (no `get_currency_rates`) on the check server with the spec's env (`scripts/fresh-clone-check.mjs:10-19, 27-28, 110-112`)<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* AC-G4's "76 with ninja disabled" contradicted C-1 | PROPOSED (build); **amended by D-417 (amendment 9, as built)**: listed-tool counts 85, 84 with `POE_NINJA_DISABLED` (amendment 9 §11; `tests/mcp/tradeQueryOptions.test.ts:87-92`, `scripts/fresh-clone-check.mjs:27`); see §5.1 for the build-guide's note on it |
| D-65 | **The `load_build_xml` wire contract is stricter, and gains `keepLevel`** (WU-06, WU-18; C-2, D-29).<br>• A load also fails (`LOAD_FAILED`) when the `<Build>` has no current `targetVersion` or when PoB opened a popup during it; the popups that load opened are closed (`pob-api/API/Handlers.lua:158-188`). `launch.promptMsg` alone doesn't fail a load. Whitespace-only `xml` fails as empty; non-string `xml` fails as `LOAD_FAILED`.<br>• Optional `keepLevel:true` keeps `characterLevel` and its auto mode through the load (`Handlers.lua:196-199`). The session's put-back after a temporary load uses it, so a read tool never re-estimates an auto-mode level (D-30). Crash recovery still reloads without it (flagged, `src/engine/session.ts:511`).<br>• Other pinned reply shapes: `new_build` → `{ok:true, result:{name}}`; `get_stats` `_meta.omitted` is always present and lists only non-scalar requested keys; `calc_with` keeps NaN/inf, encoded as null; a non-number request id isn't echoed; a non-table `params` becomes `{}`.<br>• New Lua actions beyond the C-5 list: `get_warnings` (WU-18; strips PoB colour codes, runs `BuildOutput` first) and `list_support_candidates {groupIndex, measure?}` (WU-11)<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* A PoB raise mid-`Build:Init` or a legacy `targetVersion` replied `ok:true` while the old build stayed loaded (WU-06 review) | PROPOSED (build); see §5.1 for the build-guide's note on it |
| D-66 | **Bridge client details** (WU-03, WU-07; C-4).<br>• A banner whose PoB version differs from the pin rejects with `EnginePinError` (`src/pobLuaBridge.ts:98, 255`).<br>• `EngineActionError`'s message is the engine's error text alone.<br>• After `exit`, the client waits up to 250 ms for `close` so the stderr tail is complete (`src/pobLuaBridge.ts:82`).<br>• `stop()` writes `quit`, waits 2 s (D-50), then SIGKILLs; `pid()` is exposed.<br>• The pin check's memo includes `allowUnpinned`, so an unpinned ok never satisfies a strict check (`src/engine/pinCheck.ts:205`) | PROPOSED (build) |
| D-67 | **Engine session details** (WU-08; C-3).<br>• The interface gains `current()` (AsyncLocalStorage), `ctx.stopEngine()` for `lua_stop`, and `snapshot-start`/`snapshot-end` event phases (`src/engine/session.ts:53, 87, 98`).<br>• The snapshot is discarded on a successful load, not before it.<br>• `lua_start` doesn't bump `revision`; `lua_stop` does.<br>• A load-kind call whose load succeeded but which then failed still bumps `revision` and takes a snapshot.<br>• A deliberate engine kill (`client.stop()`) is marked as a crash, not as an interrupted call.<br>• `lua_load_build {build_name}` ignores `name`; `lua_reload_build` reloads `loaded.path`<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* Pins down cases C-3 left open | PROPOSED (build) |
| D-68 | **Error-surface details** (WU-03; C-1, D-27).<br>• `get_nearby_nodes` errors only when none of the build's allocated nodes exist.<br>• The three spec-list paths error on an empty list.<br>• Truncation (C-1's 5000-char list) skips `isError` results.<br>• The lazy trade-stats preload runs before every trade tool except `get_leagues`, `trade_query_options` and `find_upgrades` (`src/server/toolRouter.ts:116, 151`), has a 15 s timeout, and is memoized even on failure (D-40)<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* `find_upgrades` does its own deadline-bounded `/data/stats` step (D-22, D-49) | PROPOSED (build) |
| D-69 | **Toolchain pin details** (WU-02; D-32, C-19).<br>• The `[plugins]` key in `mise.toml` must be the tool's backend name exactly as in `[tools]` (`"asdf:mise-plugins/mise-luaJIT"`); any other key is ignored without a warning. Verified with a SHA-swap control and a full `mise install` in a scratch data dir.<br>• `pob:fetch` exits early when `vendor/` HEAD matches the pin and tracked files are clean (`--untracked-files=no`), so untracked runtime files don't force a re-clone<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* The first key (the installed plugin's directory name) silently left the plugin unpinned | PROPOSED (build) |
| D-70 | **Trade client and limiter details** (WU-14; C-10, C-11).<br>• Each window counts sends over (t − P − 1 s, t], released at `stamp + P + 1 s` (PoB's 1 s guard, `src/trade/rateLimiter.ts:119, 316`). Adopted server state counts over (now − P, now].<br>• A wait is refused only when `wait > maxWaitMs` (30 s) or past the deadline (`rateLimiter.ts:294`), so a burst spanning exactly 30 s is admitted.<br>• Any non-abort transport failure is `network` (`src/trade/tradeClient.ts:247`).<br>• A failed `/data/stats` load isn't cached, so it is retried.<br>• Fetch with 0 or more than 10 ids throws `RangeError` (`tradeClient.ts:143`)<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* The half-open window without the guard let 4 searches into a 5 s interval whose limit is 3 (WU-14 review) | PROPOSED (build); **amended by D-461 (amendment 12, as built)**: a wait is refused when `wait > maxWaitMs`, now 60 s (`DEFAULT_MAX_WAIT_MS`, `src/trade/rateLimiter.ts`:147), not 30 s; searches also keep the pace of 45 s apart, at most 7 in 300 s (`rateLimiter.ts`:176) (amendment 12 §16; implied, as for D-22) |
| D-71 | **`search_trade_items` output** (WU-04, WU-15; D-51h, D-61, AC-T46).<br>• Always sends status `available` and sorts by price ascending (`src/handlers/tradeHandlers.ts:126, 278`).<br>• An omitted `price_currency` means exalted equivalent, not chaos.<br>• `charm` maps to trade category `flask`, after PoB's `TradeHelpers` (WU-20 confirms live).<br>• Listings show `Listing: #<id>` under one search link instead of a URL each (`tradeHandlers.ts:211`), because real ids are 194–919+ chars and break the C-1 5000-char cap.<br>• `Links: nL` becomes `Rune sockets: n` (`tradeHandlers.ts:201`); instant-buyout listings get a marker; the legend keeps only the online/offline markers.<br>• The `(~N chaos)` suffix is gone; the `Stats: +N Life, +N ES, +N% Res` line stays; normalized prices use the D-72 format (2 decimals from 1 up, 4 significant digits below 1), as `find_upgrades` and `get_item_price` do (amended during build: 2 decimals showed typical exalted listings as `≈ 0.00 divine`; `src/handlers/tradeHandlers.ts:190`) | PROPOSED (build); **amended by D-474 (amendment 13, as built)**: `search_trade_items` replies end with the pace line, handler errors included (amendment 13 §15) |
| D-72 | **Other kept trade tools** (WU-15; C-12).<br>• `get_item_price` uses the D-20 default league when none is given, prints 4 significant digits below 1, and always prints the native spread.<br>• `search_stats` uses live stats only, errors if they never loaded, and breaks ties by shorter text, then catalogue order.<br>• `get_currency_rates` errors when poe.ninja is unavailable (`src/handlers/poeNinjaHandlers.ts:22`) and drops the old preamble.<br>• poe.ninja requests time out after 15 s (`src/services/poeNinjaClient.ts:12`). `Rates` gains `nameOf` and `currencies`<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* 2 decimals showed exalted listings as 0.00/0.01 divine (WU-15 review) | PROPOSED (build); **amended by D-474 (amendment 13, as built)**: `get_item_price` replies end with the pace line, handler errors included (amendment 13 §15) |
| D-73 | **Lua trade actions details** (WU-16; C-13, D-55).<br>• `trade_evaluate_items` accepts an additive `{id, itemText}` entry form (`pob-api/API/Trade.lua:479-481`), feeds fetch entries to PoB's parser one at a time, and runs `BuildOutput()` first. Entries rejected without an id are reported as `#<index>`.<br>• Runes and anoints apply only on slots where PoB's dialog shows them; gear jewel sockets report `runeBehaviour:true`, copying PoB's substring tests (`Trade.lua:225`).<br>• Jewel `trade_query_options` uses the `Base` type. `savedWeights` follows the generator's default rule. Input validation the spec leaves open fails with `FAILED`.<br>• PoB's `FinishQuery` re-sort doesn't break ties by trade id; the result is still deterministic, given D-55's pre-sort.<br>• Every action leaves `#main.popups` unchanged (`Trade.lua:676-682`) | PROPOSED (build) |
| D-74 | **C-21's context carries the session** (WU-17; C-21 amended). The `runUpgradeSearch` ctx gains a required `session: Pick<EngineSession,'revision'>`, whose `revision` keys the generated-query cache (C-14 step 4) (`src/trade/findUpgrades.ts:213-217`)<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* The engine call context carries no revision; an optional field silently disabled the cache (WU-17 review) | PROPOSED (build) |
| D-75 | **`find_upgrades` stop and deadline semantics** (WU-17, WU-23; D-22, D-49).<br>• A request timeout counts as the deadline cut when less than 15 s of budget was left (C-10's 15 s cap).<br>• A budget cut before the query exists (step 6) is `isError`, since no link can be built; a cut at step 7 returns an early stop.<br>• `http`/`network`/`timeout` after ≥ 1 fetched listing returns "Stopped early"; with none fetched, it is `isError`.<br>• A deadline-caused `would_wait` uses the rate-limit stop text. "The engine was busy" comes from `EngineBusyError` at the session.<br>• `searches` counts every request sent, including a `too_complex` retry.<br>• Fallbacks after a search link to the last query sent; the no-session fallback links to the generated query.<br>• Deadline-bound engine steps end by abort at the deadline minus 250 ms (`src/trade/rankUpgrades.ts:52`); the clipped bridge timeout is only a backstop, because a timeout SIGKILLs the engine and loses the result.<br>• The poe.ninja rates lookup runs after evaluation, as C-14's step order has it (evaluate, then rates, then price), and only when at least one listing was evaluated; it is bounded by min(15 s, time left less the 250 ms reserve) (`src/trade/rankUpgrades.ts:388, 467-475`); a cut adds the budget stop note and keeps the rows (corrected during build: this row first said "before evaluation", which the code never did).<br>• `/data/*` loads are bounded by racing, not aborting<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* A clipped timeout killed the engine and lost the partial result (WU-23 review) | PROPOSED (build) |
| D-76 | **`find_upgrades` output wording** (WU-23; D-53).<br>• "No upgrade within budget." is always stated when no upgrade is found (`src/trade/renderUpgrades.ts:20`); closing wording exists for nothing evaluated, every search total 0, and every item rejected.<br>• Partial rejection reads `<r> of <n> fetched item(s) were rejected` (`rankUpgrades.ts:454`).<br>• The Current item line comes from `get_items` and may say `unknown` or `empty`.<br>• A listing with no whisper and no Sum gets the search link plus the seller.<br>• Session notices aren't repeated in the header (the router puts them first, C-3 step 8); a `refit(reservedChars)` hook makes them count toward the 30,000-char budget (`src/server/toolRouter.ts:97-98`)<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* Notices pushed the output past 30,000 chars (WU-23 review) | PROPOSED (build) |
| D-77 | **Engine read tools: stats, tree, passives** (WU-09, WU-10).<br>• `lua_get_stats` prints a `Tree Version:` line; its default grouping classifies by pattern with no section caps; it always shows `EvadeChance` and `DeflectChance` (at 0 too, AC-E41).<br>• `list_item_sets` labels the active set; spec tools say "points", not "nodes"; `create_spec`/`select_spec` follow up with the spec list.<br>• `get_items` refreshes sockets and slots (`ItemsTab:PopulateSlots()`); hidden slots are dropped by consumers (`src/utils/itemSlots.ts`).<br>• `search_nodes` additively returns PoB's `pathDist`/`path`.<br>• `get_passive_upgrades` measures the 8 nearest notables per keyword with `calc_with` over the whole path, ranks by gain per point, lists only improving candidates, excludes ascendancy notables (`src/handlers/treeHandlers.ts:412-426`), and propagates engine errors.<br>• `get_build_info` takes class names from the active spec first. `plan_leveling` drops the loaded ascendancy when `class_name` names another class. `reimport_nodes` keeps `masterySelections`<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* `get_passive_upgrades` suggested unallocatable ascendancy notables priced in passive points (WU-10 review) | PROPOSED (build); **amended by D-417 (amendment 9, as built)**: `get_passive_upgrades` takes every main-tree node within 6 points from the power builder as candidates, not keyword hits; its ascendancy candidates stay `calc_with` on each (amendment 9 §11); see §5.1 for the build-guide's note on it |
| D-78 | **Items, gems and skills tools** (WU-11). User-visible surface changes:<br>• `toggle_flask` takes `slot` (a Flask or Charm slot) instead of `flask_number`; an empty slot is an error (`src/server/toolSchemas.ts:579-595`).<br>• `suggest_supports` always measures; `measure_dps` and the tag heuristic are gone; `count` defaults to 8, max 20. It ranks PoB's candidate pool (legacy supports and ones already in the group dropped) by the build's gem-sort stat. `analyze_skills` lists the full pool only for the main group.<br>• `add_gem` accepts an exact case-insensitive name or a gem id. Ties: a base-type name beats the gem name, and a regular gem beats a unique variant; remaining ties are rejected with gem ids.<br>• `add_item_text`'s reply `slot` is the slot that actually holds the item.<br>• `add_gem` and `add_item_text` gain optional `validateOnly`; the multi-step tools (`setup_skill_with_gems`, `add_multiple_items`) are all-or-nothing and say so<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* Measuring in Lua takes ~1.4 s against > 60 s with round trips; partial multi-step changes diverged from the session record (WU-11 review) | PROPOSED (build); see §5.1 for the build-guide's note on it |
| D-79 | **Config tools** (WU-12; C-5, C-16).<br>• `get_config` prints every PoB2 config option (~559) as `key: value`, grouped by PoB section; unset ones show PoB's value marked `(default)` (`src/handlers/configHandlers.ts:50-53`). The reply schema gains `defaults` and `options` (additive).<br>• `set_config` rejects non-ConfigOptions keys and wrong-typed values, all-or-nothing. `""` clears a non-checkbox option; for a list it sets what PoB's `CreateConfigSet` writes (`list[defaultIndex].val`, else `defaultState`) (`pob-api/API/BuildOps.lua:337-338`). A list value differing from PoB's only by carriage returns is accepted verbatim.<br>• Presets are `{enemyLevel, input}`; loading one sends all keys in one `set_config` and clears options the preset doesn't hold.<br>• `set_enemy_stats` drops its hard-coded penetration/exposure advice (D-3)<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* `""` on a list option wrote nil, which save/reload silently reverted (WU-12 review) | PROPOSED (build); **amended by D-425 (amendment 9, as built)**: each `get_config` line reads `key: value — label`, the label PoB's Configuration tab shows, when it has one (amendment 9 §24.2, C-37c; worklog:5391, 5400); **amended by D-460 (amendment 12, as built)**: loading a preset returns a checkbox the preset doesn't hold to PoB's default, not to false, through a reserved key only `load_config_preset` sends; `set_config` still refuses `""` on a checkbox (amendment 12 §15 B6; worklog:5895-5898) |
| D-80 | **Build-file writer details** (WU-22, WU-13; C-16, C-17).<br>• The changed-on-disk check compares size + sha1 and ignores mtime (`src/services/buildFileWriter.ts:246, 288`).<br>• Backups are written exclusively; a second backup in the same timestamp gets `-1`. `restore_snapshot`'s backup goes to `<stateDir>/backups/<build>/`.<br>• `lua_save_build` checks for a `<PathOfBuilding2>` root before writing.<br>• The writer gains an optional `guard`, `checkBuildFile` and a per-target lock (additive), because `set_build_notes` and `restore_snapshot` run outside the engine lock<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* Concurrent writes shared a temp name and poisoned the fingerprint (WU-13 review) | PROPOSED (build) |
| D-81 | **Import, export, analyze and snapshot tools** (WU-13).<br>• `import_build` gains `overwrite`, since C-17 requires it to replace a file. `save_as` over the loaded file runs the changed-on-disk guard before the load; if the target changes between that check and the write, the result is `isError` with the import loaded but unsaved.<br>• `export_build`: a relative `output_directory` resolves inside the builds folder; symlinks resolve before the D-42 check; a state-dir export needs `overwrite`; a non-`<PathOfBuilding2>` source is refused even for a byte copy; notes are appended after a `---` separator (`src/services/buildExportService.ts:104`); whitespace-only notes count as empty.<br>• `analyze_build`/`compare_builds` fall back to file-only analysis with a note when the engine refuses a file. `analyze_build` no longer overrides the file's active spec or item set.<br>• Snapshots are keyed `snapshots/<rel incl .xml>/`; a same-millisecond second snapshot gets `-n`, written exclusively.<br>• `list_builds` returns sorted POSIX relative paths<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* `save_as` over the loaded file could overwrite a PoB2 save landing during the fetch (WU-13 review) | PROPOSED (build) |
| D-82 | **Advice details** (WU-18; D-30, FR-A1).<br>• The resistance judgment includes Chaos (`src/advice/judge.ts:17`).<br>• A gear slot is any shown, active slot whose name lacks "Jewel" (`judge.ts:55-57`).<br>• The load summary's Top Issues lists every issue, not the first 3, so it equals `get_build_issues` (AC-A4).<br>• A `build_name` resolving to the loaded build analyses the engine's build as is (`src/advice/report.ts:76`).<br>• `analyze_build`'s validation section uses `judge()` and reads named stat fields; Crit Multi shows `CritMultiplier × 100` there and in `compare_builds` (was always N/A) | PROPOSED (build) |
| D-83 | **README tool table** (WU-19; AC-G1). `pnpm docs:tools` generates it from the registry; each row's one-line description is the description's first sentence (`e.g.`/`i.e.`/`etc.`/`vs.` don't end it), with pipe characters escaped and `< >` as HTML entities (`scripts/gen-tool-table.mjs:7, 36`). README setup says "installs the dependencies with pnpm from the lockfile", because "pnpm install" contains AC-G2's banned "npm install"; `CLAUDE.md` keeps the literal bootstrap | PROPOSED (build) |
| D-84 | **`trade_generate_query` checks `report_stats` before generating** (final pass; C-13 amended, C-14 step 1, D-27). `find_upgrades` passes its `report_stats` to `trade_generate_query` as a new optional `reportStats` param (additive), which checks each name by `trade_evaluate_items`' rule before anything is generated (`pob-api/API/Trade.lua:338-360, 429-431`; `src/trade/findUpgrades.ts:184`). A bad name is `isError` naming `report_stats.<i>`, and no trade request is sent<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* `report_stats` was only checked at evaluation, after the search and fetch had spent rate-limit slots (final-pass skeptic T-F1) | PROPOSED (build) |
| D-85 | **A `find_upgrades` deadline bounds the session's own steps, not only the lock wait** (final pass; C-3 amended, D-22, D-75, NFR-4). In C-3 terms these are steps 2–4: the engine start, the crash restore and the disk-change reload.<br>• No step starts with less than 1 s left; the engine start is raced against the deadline.<br>• The restore and the reload get min(default timeout, time left) and end by abort 250 ms before the deadline, so the engine is not killed (`src/engine/session.ts:687`).<br>• A cut step throws `EngineDeadlineError` and leaves the books (snapshot, crash flag, `loaded`) for the next call; `find_upgrades` renders it as the budget stop, `isError` since no query exists yet.<br>• Query generation (C-14 step 6) likewise ends by the shared deadline abort, not by the bridge timeout's SIGKILL (`src/trade/findUpgrades.ts:598`)<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* A slow start or reload ran past the 90 s budget, and a cut generation killed the engine (final-pass skeptic T-F2, T-F3) | PROPOSED (build) |
| D-86 | **The changed-on-disk guard knows the server's own writes and every file the engine held** (final pass; C-17 step 3 amended, FR-E4).<br>• A text, byte or restore write to the loaded file also accepts the writer's own last write to it, so the server's earlier `set_build_notes` or `restore_snapshot` is not taken for a PoB2 save. An engine save still compares with `loaded.fingerprint` only, so it never clobbers that edit.<br>• The session reports every fingerprint the loaded file gets (a load, the disk check's refresh or reload, an engine save) to the writer (`onFileFingerprint`, `src/engine/session.ts:134`), which keeps the last one for the last 32 paths (`src/services/buildFileWriter.ts:278`). A write to a file that is no longer loaded compares with the later of that record and the writer's own last write, so loading another build, a new build, an import or a Save As re-point no longer drops the guard on the file the engine held<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* Back-to-back server edits were refused as "changed on disk" (final-pass skeptic S-F1); after a build switch, an overwrite of the previous file skipped the check even when PoB2 had saved it since (final-pass tracer) | PROPOSED (build) |
| D-87 | **The limiter never sends after a wait with less than 1 s before the deadline** (final pass; C-11 amended, D-22, R-1). Admission refuses a wait that would end with < 1 s left, and after any wait (the sleep, which can overshoot, or waiting for a class's first request) the limiter re-checks and refuses with `would_wait` without sending (`src/trade/rateLimiter.ts:125, 226-245`). Without a wait it does not refuse; the caller checks between steps<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* A request sure to be cut by the deadline still spends an account rate-limit slot (final-pass tracer) | PROPOSED (build) |
| D-88 | **`export_build` never writes into the server's `backups/` or `snapshots/`** (final pass; D-42, D-81 refined). An `output_directory` inside `<stateDir>/backups` or `<stateDir>/snapshots`, directly, through `..` or through a symlink, is `isError` naming that folder, even with `overwrite: true` (`src/services/buildExportService.ts:79, 156`). The rest of the state dir stays allowed<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* With `overwrite: true` an export could replace a writer backup or a snapshot copy (final-pass tracer) | PROPOSED (build); see §5.1 for the build-guide's note on it |
| D-89 | **Deadlines and elapsed time run on a clock that never steps** (final pass; D-22). The server's `now` defaults to `performance.timeOrigin + performance.now()` (`src/utils/clock.ts`), epoch-aligned and monotonic; the router's arrival time, the session, the trade client, the limiter and every `find_upgrades` deadline check use it (`ctx.now`, `src/trade/findUpgrades.ts:515`). Wall-clock uses (cache ages, timestamps, mtimes, file names) keep `Date.now()`. Tests measure durations with `performance.now()`<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* The WSL2 wall clock stepped backwards mid-test (AC-E8 measured -2431 ms); a step mid-call would stretch or cut a deadline | PROPOSED (build) |
| D-90 | **Short exact-item `Buy:` links** (found in the user's WU-20 smoke, 2026-10-06; replaces D-39's exact-item recipe; WU-24).<br>• **Why.** PoB's exact-item link repeats the whole weighted query, about 4,000 chars per link. Only about 6 listings fit in full under the D-53 cap, compact entries carried no link, and the client model left the URLs out of its summary.<br>• **The new link** uses the same URL form and league encoding as D-39, over a minimal query that pins the listing: `{query: {status: {option: <the search's status>}, name: <item name, omitted when empty>, type: <base type>, filters: {trade_filters: {filters: {account: {input: <seller account>}, price: {min: <amount>, max: <amount>, option: <listing currency>}}}}}}`. There is no `stats` array.<br>• **Compact entries** also carry the link: the D-53 compact line ends with ` · Buy: <whisper or link>`.<br>• **The `find_upgrades` description** tells the client to show each listing's `Buy:` line verbatim.<br>• **Accepted risk.** One seller listing two items with the same name, base and price will open both.<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* The links must survive a model's summary and fit the budget | HUMAN (2026-10-06) |
| D-91 | **New tool `set_gem_corruption`** (user request, 2026-10-06; HG-3 resolved by the user; WU-25). It mirrors PoB2's gem corruption dropdown (`SkillsTab.lua` `corruptOption`):<br>• **`state`:** `not_corrupted` (`corrupted=false`, `corruptLevel=0`), `corrupted` (`true`, `0`), `plus_one_level` (`true`, `+1`) or `minus_one_level` (`true`, `−1`).<br>• **Scope:**<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;– `group_index` + `gem_index`: that gem;<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;– only `group_index`: every active skill gem in that socket group;<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;– neither: every active skill gem in the build's user socket groups.<br>• **Support gems can't be corrupted in PoE2** (the user). A scope never changes them, and naming a support gem explicitly is `isError` with nothing changed.<br>• **Engine kind and save.** The kind is `mutate`; the state round-trips through the build XML (`corrupted`, `corruptLevel`).<br>• **Tool counts** become 77, or 76 with `POE_NINJA_DISABLED`.<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* PoB2's UI offers it, and armour such as "+1 to Level of all Corrupted Skill Gems" needs it | HUMAN (2026-10-06); see §5.1 for the build-guide's note on it |
| D-92 | **What the build of WU-24 and WU-25 decided** (2026-10-06).<br>• **Link name.** The D-90 link's name comes from the fetched item's own `name`, so magic and normal items have none.<br>• **Hard cap.** If even the compact lines with their `Buy:` would pass 30,000 chars, the last compact lines drop their `Buy:`. The D-53 cap stays hard; this can't happen with trade-site-length links.<br>• **Corruption scope.** `set_gem_corruption` scopes cover only *user* socket groups, those without a PoB `source` attribute. An explicit item- or passive-granted group is `isError`.<br>• **Which gems count as support.** A gem whose primary granted effect is a support counts as a support (e.g. Fiery Death), even when it also grants an active effect.<br>• **Empty scope.** A scope with no active skill gems is `isError`, with nothing changed.<br>*Rationale / rejected alternatives:* `src/trade/links.ts`, `src/trade/renderUpgrades.ts`, `pob-api/API/BuildOps.lua` `isActiveSkillGem` | PROPOSED (build) |

| D-211 | **The write ledger.** The server keeps `guide-writes.json` in its state dir (C-16): per builds-relative path, `{path, sha1, runFolder, writtenAt}` from the last final compile that wrote it. No agent can write there (D-177). A final compile may overwrite an existing builds-folder file only when the ledger's entry for that path names the plan's run folder **and** the file's current sha1 equals the entry's, i.e. nobody edited it since. Otherwise it's refused: with C-17's changed-on-disk wording when this run's entry no longer matches the file, and with C-17's "already exists" wording when the ledger has no entry for this run (D-113). Measure-only compiles never touch the ledger or the PoB file. Order: check, write the PoB file through C-17, write the ledger entry, write the record; a failure after the PoB write still leaves the ledger entry, so a retry can overwrite. `compile-record.json` is informational. Basis: C-17's in-memory guard doesn't survive a restart and is refreshed by an auto-reload; the record is agent-editable; a measure-only compile rewrote the record and so blocked the run's final re-compile. **Amends D-199** | review fix (S3); **amended by D-466 (amendment 13, as built)**: the ledger (version 2) keeps drafts too, each entry with `kind` (`final`/`draft`) and `plan`; a version-1 entry reads as a final with no `plan`; a deleted draft's entry is dropped; a name is free when this build (`plan`) wrote it unchanged (amendment 13 §7, Appendix A); **amended by D-479 (amendment 14, as built)**: entries gain `baseSha1` (the sha1 of the bytes a compile or draft wrote; an entry without it takes its `sha1`) and `adopted`; format stays version 2; **amended by D-487 (amendment 14, as built)**: the notes write updates `sha1`, never `baseSha1` (amendment 14 §5, §13, Appendix A) |
| D-212 | **Config placeholders are values.** PoB applies a numeric option's placeholder when it has no input (`BuildModList`, pin `Classes/ConfigTab.lua:883-900`), the enemy level's through `UpdateLevel` (`:870-880`), and `enemyIsBoss` sets the placeholders of the enemy's level, damage, resistances, penetration, armour, evasion, speed and crit (`Modules/ConfigOptions.lua:1964-1997`). So the default audit (C-32) covers placeholder-driven options: each one that affects calcs is either set explicitly per stage from C1/E1 (a scenario input) or listed in `data/config-baseline.json` with a rationale (D-230). The compile record stores each stage's effective values, explicit, default or placeholder, with their class. PoB's Save writes no `<Input>` equal to its placeholder (`ConfigTab.lua:712-714, :741-742`), so the record, not the XML, is authoritative (D-113). Corrects slice 2's A-P4 | review fix (S4); amended by D-248; its baseline rule (D-230) amended by D-250, D-252 |
| D-213 | **Tree connectivity follows PoB's weapon-set rule.** The preflight applies `CanPathThroughAllocMode` (pin `Classes/PassiveSpec.lua:826-830`): an untagged node paths only through untagged nodes; a node tagged weapon set n also through nodes tagged n. `compile_stage` reads back PoB's allocation with its tags, and the compile fails naming any node PoB dropped, added or re-tagged, because PoB prunes unconnected nodes silently | review fix (S5); **extended by D-360 (v1.5)**: `tree-shape`, one start exit per tree and one tree per start; **amended by D-364 (v1.6)**: a tree may touch two exits of its start through its own nodes, so "one start exit per tree" goes; `tree-shape` keeps one tree per start, the alternate start and the ascendancy tree |

| D-377 | **A group chooses its minion's skill** (amendment 6 §3). | D-382 | **The plan's groups are found at PoB's own index** (the Varashta run's blocker). On every MAIN-mode calculation PoB appends a group, with a `source`, for each granted skill that has no group yet, and removes stale ones (`Modules/CalcSetup.lua:1501-1663`). So a plan group's position isn't its `socketGroupList` index: a stage with an off-hand calculates before its skills are built, and every plan index in the run's skeletons was off by one, measuring PoB's own level-1 Navira. Now the plan's groups are the active skill set's groups without a `source`, in order, and every use reads PoB's index from that list (`select_minion_skills`, `select_main`, `granted_skills`, `stage_gems`). Every group number in `compile_stage`'s reply stays the plan's: the plan's groups first, in plan order, then PoB's own, in PoB's order, which is the contract the record, V1, the ledger refs, the D1 gate and the checker already assumed. The stage's skill set is selected with its tree and items, before its first calculation, so PoB's groups for this stage land in its own set. Not changed: PoB's group order, and when PoB calculates | PROPOSED by the coordinator, 2026-10-17; built; refines D-310, C-41; **amended by D-435 (amendment 10, as built)**: once a stage's groups are built and calculated, `compile_stage` reorders the active skill set's list, the plan's groups first in plan order, then PoB's own (`plan_groups_first`); "Not changed: PoB's group order" no longer holds. Already in slice 02 (`f1b4e94`); worklog:5512-5520 (as built; the user, 2026-10-23: "Keep it") |
| D-383 | **Unlock constraints** (the defect batch). A node with an `unlockConstraint` is allocatable only while every node it names is allocated (`Classes/PassiveSpec.lua:1248-1251`; 197 of the pin's 200 constraints name the Oracle's The Unseen Path, 5571). `compile_check` refuses such a node, naming the node that unlocks it; V1 needs no rule, since a refused plan never compiles. A new read action, `get_rule_breaks`, lists a loaded build's locked nodes; `judge()` reports them after PoB's warnings, so `validate_build`, `get_build_issues` and the load summary name them | PROPOSED by the coordinator, 2026-10-17 (the Varashta run's gap list, item 1); built; amends C-41, FR-A1 |

| D-390 | **How the runner answers a question** (amendment 7 §3). A research question goes to a `build-guide-researcher` (the lookups, and the web online; up to 3 in parallel); a question about a compiled build, or one that needs a measurement, goes to a `build-guide-coordinator`, which runs alone, may compile a variant under `probes/q-<gate>-<visit>-<n>/`, and never edits a plan or a card. The step writes `interviews/<gate>-<visit>-q<n>.md` and returns at most 8 lines; the runner gives the user the answer and the path, and logs stage `Q`. | D-391 | **A choice can carry changes, or make a new card** (amendment 7 §4). The runner sorts each change the user asks for into a revision, which keeps the card's identity (its class, ascendancy and named damage source) and the core of its chain and adds or swaps supporting pieces (one may come from another card), or a new card: a different main skill, ascendancy or engine, another card's core mechanic, or two cards' ideas combined. At the verification gate a revision rides on the finalized card as D1-build's `user-request` findings, and a new card goes to a verification round. As built: a pick of new cards only is written `pick:` with no id; R1b's header carries `changes`; a breaking change fails R1b with `gate-change`; a verification `pick:`'s changes need no card line; a new card may draw on a card under "Not dealt" | settled (the user, 2026-10-18: a "new card for change requests that aren't a straightforward revision of an existing card"); built, WU-81, WU-82; amends D-287, D-319; **amended by D-432 (amendment 10, as built)**: a gate-made card's `from` may name cards, composites and piece keys; one that draws on pieces is a composite, with Pieces tables and leave-one-out probes (amendment 10 §7) |

| D-417 | **Node power as a tool, `get_node_power` (C-55)** (a9 §11). Group `core`, engine `read`, on the loaded build. Arguments: `stats` (1 to 3 of PoB's node-power stats with a `stat`, minion versions included, "Offence/Defence" refused; the first ranks; default `["FullDPS", "TotalEHP"]`), `max_loss` (default 2% per other stat, 0 for the guards: movement speed and the clear entry's radius and uses per second), `clear_group`, `protect_for` (later rungs' loadouts), `max_distance` (default 6, at most 12), `loadout`, `level`, `keep`, `limit` (default 15; 0 is compact mode). One engine request (PoB's `PowerBuilder` computation once, every stat read from each output), a 120 s long action. It selects the loadout and level inside the call and restores them; the build and `revision` are unchanged. Returns the best unallocated nodes by path gain per point, the weakest allocated branches by loss per point, the travel count, and the best legal swap (searched on a scratch tree after removing each of the 5 weakest unprotected branches; connected, within its points, no second tree on a start, no weapon-set or ascendancy node), plus C-47's can't-measure lines. The swap loop, in ladder order once each rung's floor holds: `get_node_power` (compact) with `keep` = the needs (plus the rung before's nodes for `b` and `n`), `clear_group`, `protect_for` = the later rungs; apply the best swap up the ladder, compile the rungs it touches, undo it if any floor fails or V1 gains a finding; an undone swap's nodes become a floor need; spare points go to the best per-point nodes; repeat while a swap gains ≥3%, at most 8 swaps per rung. | D-510 | **PG-106, the gem-group recount** (a16 §6). The game's rule: an enabled group counts against the skill slots when its first enabled active gem (not a support) isn't granted, that is, neither the gem nor its granted effect carries `fromItem` or `fromTree`; so a support in a granted skill no longer counts its group, and a group with no enabled active gem counts nothing. A group the compile marks switched (D-518) counts as if enabled, since a gem switched off in the game still fills a slot. The patch wraps `common.classes.SkillsTab.UpdateGlobalGemCountAssignments`: it calls PoB's method, so PoB's own bookkeeping stays, then overwrites `GlobalGemAssignments.GemGroupCount` with the recount; both callers reach it (PoB's `UpdateGemSlots` and D-428's refresh). Only PoB's warning past its skill slots moves, which V1's `skill-slots`, `get_build_issues` and the advice report read; no figure moves. | D-512 | **PG-114, a granted skill's quality** (a16 §8). The game's facts (choice E, the user's words): a granted skill gains base quality only on a wand, a staff or a sceptre, whose own quality, raised with Arcanist's Etchers, is the skill's (quarterstaves, PoB's `Warstaff` staves, pass nothing); a skill another item or a passive grants has no quality of its own; skill level and quality modifiers, local and global, apply to every granted skill; an item's granted skill has as many support slots as its item's level gives, Jeweller's Orbs on the item adding more, and a passive's as many as character level gives (D-359's estimate). ## 8. Autonomy envelope

Citations in §8–§10: `worklog:n` and `WL:n` are lines of `docs/build-guide/worklog.md`, `stab worklog:n` lines of
`docs/stabilization/worklog.md`; "amendment N §n" is a section of that amendment's file.

### 8.1 Settled (proceed, don't relitigate)

**The server (from the stabilization spine).** Everything in §7, every `C-n` in §5, and the vision's §4 tool-disposition table.

**The build-guide (from the build-guide spine).** Everything in §7 and every contract in §5. The server's decisions (§7) and contracts (§5), except where §5.1 amends them.

### 8.2 Assumptions (proceed-on)

| ID | Assumption | Basis | If wrong |
|---|---|---|---|
| A-1 | trade2 request and response shapes are as in `tests/fixtures/trade2/` | 36 captured fixture files, logged-in ones included | WU-20 smoke catches it → HG-5 |
| A-2 | Rate-limit triples are `max:window:penalty` and state triples are `hits:window:activePenaltyS` | GGG docs + PoB + fixtures | — |
| A-3 | trade2's complexity budget is ~200, and D-23's model over-estimates. Observed vs model: belt 9 vs 23; ring 9 vs 23; body-armour 11 vs 11; and-3 12 vs 14; weighted-session 180 vs 191 | 5 fixture data points | D-23's single retry covers it |
| A-4 | Fetch takes ≤ 10 ids per request | PoB block size; never exceeded | — |
| A-5 | `Retry-After` is integer seconds | GGG docs | — |
| A-6 | An expired or invalid cookie is treated as anonymous (400 code 2 without `Account` on a weighted query) | inference (S-4) | WU-20 records the real response |
| A-7 | poe.ninja's `league` parameter accepts trade league names | our client and PoB both pass trade names | rates unavailable → D-11 fallback; WU-20 checks |
| A-8 | Claude Code sends a `progressToken` on `tools/call` | docs imply it (ctx-mcp C7) | no progress; harmless |
| A-9 | `fetch` results for weighted queries carry `pseudoMods[0]` "Sum: <w>" | fixture `fetch-weighted-session` (10/10) | — |
| A-10 | The trade server's price filter with no `option` means Exalted-equivalent, using the server's own rates | PoB default; S-4 | the D-11 post-filter catches disagreements |
| A-11 | Windows PoB2 saves change `size` or `mtimeMs` as seen from WSL | `/mnt/c` is mounted 9p with `cache=5` (mmap, no metadata cache; kernel v6.6 9p doc), so `stat` isn't cached | WU-20 item 6 verifies; if wrong, hash with sha1 on every engine call |
| A-12 | Golden values: the fixture's 109 `PlayerStat` pairs are the truth at pin `v0.23.1` | WU-01 verification | HG-4 |
| A-13 | For a **weighted** query sent with a cookie, a 400 code 2 with `Account` in the rules means over-complexity, and without it means the session wasn't recognised | S-4 (200 with `Account,Ip`); 3 anonymous fixtures (400 with `Ip`) | WU-20 records what it sees |
| A-14 | poe.ninja `core.rates[c]` = units of `c` per 1 primary | Fixture arithmetic: 440.6 × 0.00227 ≈ 1 | `lines[].primaryValue` (documented) is the cross-check |

A-36…A-49 are amendment 11 §20's proceed-on assumptions (approved with it, 2026-10-25) and A-50…A-62 amendment 12 §25's (approved with it, 2026-10-28), numbered at the fold in amendment order. "If wrong" gives the amendment's own answer, or its named risk. A-63…A-68 are amendment 13 §25's (approved with it, 2026-10-30) and A-69…A-75 amendment 14 §28's (approved with it, 2026-11-02) that no decision row states; the rest of those two lists are in their decisions' text (D-463…D-495) and risks. Amendment 15 §19's proceed-on assumptions (approved with it, 2026-11-06) are all stated in its decision rows (D-499…D-506) and the fold rule, so it adds no A-row. Amendment 16 §30's proceed-on assumptions (approved with it, 2026-11-08) are each marked in the section that makes it and stated in its decision rows (D-507…D-525) and contracts (C-59…C-66), so it adds no A-row either.

### 8.3 Human-decision gates (stop and escalate, `blocked:needs-human`)

The server's HG-1…HG-9 (the first rows below) stay in force. In particular HG-1, which D-297 (as D-335 restates it) narrows to
a live request to pathofexile.com or any request that carries a cookie or personal data (polite requests to
poe.ninja's documented economy endpoints need no approval; its builds, profiles and character API is never
used), HG-6 (the user's builds folder; it now covers the user's guides folder too) and HG-8 (signing).

| ID | Condition | Why a human | Unblocks with |
|---|---|---|---|
| HG-1 | A unit needs a live request to pathofexile.com or poe.ninja | Shared IP/account limits; the user's cookie | User approval, then the coordinator runs it |
| HG-2 | Any change to `pob-pin.json` (a tag bump) | Golden values move; upgrade procedure | User decision (out of scope for this epic) |
| HG-3 | Any change to the tool set beyond the vision's §4 disposition table, or to a tool's user-visible semantics beyond its row and D-51 | UX scope | User decision |
| HG-4 | Golden values fail on an unmodified pinned checkout | The environment or the fixture is broken; not a code bug | The coordinator investigates with the user |
| HG-5 | Observed trade2 behaviour contradicts the fixtures or an assumption A-1…A-15 in a way that changes a contract | Contract change | The coordinator updates the spine, re-reviews, and the user approves |
| HG-6 | A test or agent would write anywhere in the user's real builds folder, or delete any user file | Data safety | Never automatic. **amended by HG-27 (amendment 13, as built)**: for M-26 only, one read of the user's guides folder: WU-127's byte-equal copy of the Titan Rake run's two plans into the test fixtures (sha1s at worklog:6058–6059); likewise HG-32 and HG-33 (amendment 14) for WU-136's copy of the open-spell run's two plans, for M-27 only. **amended by HG-39 (amendment 16, approved)**: for WU-168's runs only (choice G), the pieces' real cases write their own folders in the user's guides folder and the better round's draft and final PoB files into the builds folder under their own names, through `buildFileWriter`, never over an existing file (a16 §30) |
| HG-7 | Claude Code can't connect to the server (for example the v2 client runtime changes negotiation) | Host-environment fact | The user tries `MCP_SDK_GENERATION=v1` or approves an SDK upgrade |
| HG-8 | A commit or push needs signing or credentials an agent can't provide | Credentials | D-48 sign-rebase with the user present |
| HG-9 | WU-20 or WU-21 acceptance (live smoke, final sign-off) | Only the user can run with their cookie and PoB2 on Windows | The user's work-log entry |
| HG-10 | Any dependency change (`package.json`, lockfile) | gap 33; the server's dependency rule (§10.1) | The user's decision |
| HG-11 | Any change to `pob-pin.json` | Golden values move; D-140 waits for the next PoB2 release | The user, after a PoB2 release |
| HG-12 | Any tool-set or tool-contract change beyond C-37…C-50 and D-143/D-174/D-175/D-202/D-216/D-360 (D-360's path-tool change was decided by the user with amendment 4, 2026-10-11). Granted since, each for its build only, by the user's acceptance of the amendment: D-372's `find_upgrades` header and stops (WU-74), D-376…D-380's minion-skill changes (M-17) and D-397's figure of a plain summon (WU-80); by the user's go-ahead for the M-19 fix batch, D-398's load warning. By the user's grant of 2026-10-18 (amendment 8 §18), for M-20 only: the plan schema's `companion` field and the compile that reads it (D-402, WU-87), and `compile_plan`'s record gaining `clearSpeed` and movement speed on every compile, card skeletons included (D-407, WU-88); no tool is added and no argument changes. By the user's go-ahead of 2026-10-23 (amendment 9 §24.2), for M-22 only: `search_tree_nodes`' `from` (C-43a); `lookup_game_data`'s gem levels' costs and reservation and its `base`, `config` and `constant` modes, with `get_config`'s labels (C-37a…C-37d); `list_gems`' `skill_type` (C-56); `get_node_power`'s pool moves (C-55a); the plan's `skills.groups[].weaponSet` and the record's `skillOutputsSet2`, `outputsSet2` and `travel` (C-41). By the user's direction of 2026-10-23 (amendment 10's header and §10), for M-23 only: D-435's base-implicit rule (the compiler fills a bare base's implicits; V1's `implicit-variant`). By the user's approval of amendment 12 (2026-10-28, HG-23; amendment 12 §15, §16, §24), for M-25 only: D-460's tool fixes (the contract error's union paths, B1, B2, B3's radius jewels in `compile_plan`'s connection check, B4, B5, B6, and B8 with `find_path_to_node`, `update_tree_delta`'s `autoPathedNames`) and D-461's trade pace (45 s between searches and 7 in 300 s, the 60 s wait cap, `find_upgrades`' two searches a call and its stop line, the 429 log), with plan schema 8 and the record's additions (D-449, D-451, D-452, D-454, D-455). By the user's approval of amendment 13 (2026-10-30, HG-27), for M-26 only: node power's new arguments and guards, `free` on a pool node and Split Personality's start (C-55); `compile_plan`'s draft mode, `remove_draft`, `first_free` names and its schema-9 behaviour (C-41); `buildFileWriter`'s delete and the write ledger's version 2 (C-17, D-211); the trade tools' pace line (C-14, D-71, D-72); the path tools from Split Personality's start (C-48); `compile_stage`'s 120 s long action (C-4, NFR-21); and plan schema 9 (C-31). By the user's approval of amendment 14 (2026-11-02, HG-32), for M-27 only: `compile_plan`'s read-back, adopt, notes and trade-links modes, `final-build.xml`, `groupCount` on every counted group, and V1's `affix-cap`, `sockets` and `ascendancy-jewel` (C-41); the record's `chains` and `projectiles` (C-33); the ledger's `baseSha1` and `adopted`, the writer's adoption and `set_build_notes`' refresh (C-17); node power's `exclude`, attribute repair and its illegal form, attribute choice, 0.5 tolerance and rounded reasons (C-55); `find_upgrades`' `link_only`, listing tiers and the fallback's prefixes (C-14); the lookup's tier levels, base socket line and generated-variant filter (C-37); the bridge's parse fix (C-6); plan schema 10 (C-31); `data/items/sockets.json`, `data/items/item-only-skills.json` and the loader's `items` folder (C-35). The defect batch's changes to existing tools (D-383's and D-384's refusals, left-out lists and warnings, D-387's emotion prices, D-388's ranker field) were made as defect fixes and confirmed by the user afterwards (2026-10-18, "I approve the sign off on the changes in the fold").
